### ~~RLS + RPC authorization~~ — ✅ **SERIES CLOSED. ALL SEVEN PARTS (0, 1, 2, 3, 4a, 4b, 5) SHIPPED AND VERIFIED ON DEV + PROD** → see DONE *(NEW 2026-07-30; UPDATED 2026-07-31; 2026-08-03 Part 1 (`030`) live; 2026-08-17 Part 4b (`032`) live; **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — Parts 5 (`034`), 3 (`035`), 2 (`036`+`037`) all live on dev + prod, every part verified from OUTSIDE the SQL editor**)*

> **✅ 2026-08-17 — THE SERIES IS CLOSED.** Nothing from `SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md` remains open. Kept here only until the next sprint rolls; the detail lives in DONE. **Two NEW, narrower authorization items opened tonight and are tracked separately below — `create_household` (NEXT) and the 21-function anon-exec sweep (LATER).** Neither is part of this spec, and neither blocks meal planning.

> **✅ 2026-08-17 — PART 4b SHIPPED to dev AND prod (`032`).** RLS enabled on all three cycle tables with SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE policies and **no DELETE policy** (deliberate — matches `household_members`). **The spec's fix block was wrong:** `known_stores` and `shopping_sessions` already carried three **dormant** policies each (`archive/005`, repaired `014`), encoding per-user session writes and `deleted_at` SELECT filters. Since permissive policies **OR** together, creating the spec'd set alongside them would have widened access, not narrowed it — the existing design was preserved and only `provision_cycles` got net-new policies. Two real gaps repaired: `sessions_update_own` had **no membership check at all**; it and `known_stores_update_household` both had `USING` with no `WITH CHECK`. **⚠️ `shopping_sessions` policies are verified by inspection only** — `startSession()` is never called from `App.js`, so no UI path exercises them. **Remaining: Parts 2, 3, 5.**

> **✅ 2026-08-17 — PARTS 5 (`034`) + 3 (`035`) SHIPPED to dev AND prod.** *(Superseded the "authored, apply pending" note below — both applied and verified the same night.)* Apply was a manual SQL-editor step throughout: the Supabase MCP connects as `supabase_read_only_user` in *both* environments (`ERROR 25006: cannot execute ALTER FUNCTION in a read-only transaction`) and exposes no `apply_migration`. **`034`** pins `search_path = public, extensions` on the five unpinned SECURITY DEFINER functions; the `proconfig IS NULL` set is identical on dev and prod, and none of the five bodies references `auth.*`, `storage.*`, `extensions.*` or earthdistance, so the pin is behaviour-neutral (checked before authoring, not assumed). **`035`** drops `household_members_insert`. Its safety rests on the RPC path bypassing RLS, which was **verified rather than assumed**: `household_members` is owned by `postgres` with **`relforcerowsecurity = false`**, and all inserting functions are SECURITY DEFINER owned by `postgres`. Had FORCE RLS been set, `035` would have locked every user out of joining. No client surface — both `household_members` references (`useProvisions.js:514`, `:1456`) are selects; there is no `.insert()` in `src/`.

> **⚠️ 2026-08-17 — THE ADVISOR BACKLOG WAS MIS-ATTRIBUTED. "~59 warnings ≈ the Part 5 `search_path` gaps" IS WRONG — only 5 of them are.** Measured directly on both databases (identity-proved via `pg_control_system().system_identifier`, not server label):
>
> | lint | dev | prod |
> |---|---|---|
> | `authenticated_security_definer_function_executable` | 25 | 26 |
> | `anon_security_definer_function_executable` | 23 | 25 |
> | `function_search_path_mutable` | **5** | **5** |
> | `rls_disabled_in_public` | 0 | 0 |
> | **total** | **53** | **56** |
>
> **`034` clears exactly one row of that table — the 5.** It does not touch the other ~48/51. **The real backlog is 23–25 SECURITY DEFINER functions executable by `anon`, and that is PART 2's surface, not Part 5's.** It is materially larger than this roadmap previously implied, and it is the no-credential class — the same severity bracket that inverted the fix order back in July. `rls_disabled_in_public` is **0 on both**, re-confirmed as a sanity check (Part 4b holding). Treat the advisor total as a Part 2 sizing input from here on.

> **✅ 2026-08-17 — PART 2 SHIPPED to dev AND prod (`036` + `037`), closing the series.** `bootstrap_new_user` no longer accepts `p_clerk_id`; identity derives from `auth.jwt()->>'sub'`. Shipped as a **zero-downtime split** — `036` adds the hardened 3-arg alongside the still-live 4-arg, then the client deploys, then `037` drops the 4-arg — because dropping a signature breaks every in-flight caller, and bootstrap runs on **every sign-in**, not just signups. Run in full on dev (three cold sign-ups: plain, valid-invite, **expired-invite falling through gracefully**) before repeating on prod. Closure proved from outside the database: the forged shape `{"p_clerk_id":…}` returns `PGRST202`, with a **control call** at `create_household` reaching its function body to prove the block is signature-specific. Prod advisor `anon_security_definer_function_executable` **25 → 21**; `function_search_path_mutable` **5 → 0**. The pre-ship analysis below is retained as the reasoning that shaped the split.
>
> **⚠️ 2026-08-17 (pre-ship analysis) — PART 2 IS NOT SQL-ONLY, AND PROD CARRIES THREE OVERLOADS DEV DOES NOT.** Prod has **four** `bootstrap_new_user` overloads; dev has **one**. This is not undocumented drift: `000_canonical_baseline.sql:401-406` states the three dead overloads — `(text,text)`, `(text,text,boolean)`, `(text,text,text)` — are *intentionally dropped in the baseline*. Dev matches canonical; **prod is the environment that never received the drop.** The advisor diff corroborates it exactly — prod's three extra warnings in *both* SECURITY DEFINER categories are those three overloads, still `anon`-executable on prod today. **One portable migration covers both environments** — `drop function if exists` no-ops cleanly on an absent signature, so prod drops three real functions and dev's clauses are harmless no-ops. Do **not** write environment-specific migrations. **The DROPs must precede the CREATE in the same transaction:** the hardened replacement signature is `(text,text,text)`, type-identical to the legacy overload, and Postgres refuses to rename a parameter via `CREATE OR REPLACE`. Part 2 also requires a **coordinated client edit** — `useProvisions.js:312-317` passes `p_clerk_id` explicitly and must stop doing so in the same commit.

| Priority | Feature | Spec |
|---|---|---|
| **P0 — NOW (Parts 4a + 0 on dev AND prod; **Part 1 on DEV ONLY** (030) — 2026-07-31; Parts 2, 3, 4b, 5 outstanding)** | **Close five authorization defects found by the first MCP-connected look at prod.** **Fix order was INVERTED by measurement — see the callout below and the spec's Ordering section. Credential requirement first, regression risk second. Shipped: 4a → 0 → 1.**, one scoped commit each, dev-green before prod. **(1) `join_household` performs NO invite validation** — it takes a bare `p_household_id` and joins the caller to it; every invite check (`not found` / `already used` / `expired`) lives in client JS at `useProvisions.js:1114-1116`. One console call joins any household by UUID. Fix: replace the `uuid` signature with `join_household(p_invite_code text)` validating server-side with the predicate that already exists in `bootstrap_new_user`; revoke `anon`. **(2) `bootstrap_new_user` trusts a client-supplied `p_clerk_id`** — SECURITY DEFINER, `anon` EXECUTE, so an unauthenticated caller can overwrite any user's email/full_name and, with any valid invite, join a household **as them**. Fix: derive from `auth.jwt()->>'sub'`, drop the parameter, drop the three dead overloads (four exist, all anon-granted), restore the missing `SET search_path`. ⚠️ `CREATE OR REPLACE` will NOT work — the new `(text,text,text)` signature is type-identical to an existing overload and Postgres refuses to rename params; the DROPs must land first. **(3) `household_members_insert` is `WITH CHECK (true)`** — drop it (not scope it) once the RPCs own joining. **(4) RLS disabled on `known_stores`, `provision_cycles`, `shopping_sessions`** in **both dev and prod**, full CRUD to `anon`+`authenticated`. All three carry `household_id NOT NULL` → direct `is_member_of()`, no join path. SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE only, **no DELETE policy**. ⚠️ **Highest regression risk of the four despite being SQL-only** — the only part touching the core loop (7 call sites: `useProvisions.js` 224/690/834/906/963 cycles, 878/923 sessions); enable+policies MUST be one migration. ~~**Blocked on:** `is_member_of()` carries the same soft-delete gap (checks `household_members.deleted_at`, not `households.deleted_at`) so membership in a deleted household satisfies **every** policy in the schema — resolve before Part 4 ships.~~ **✅ CORRECTED 2026-08-17 — this was the THIRD copy of a stale claim** (also in the spec and `ARCHITECTURE.md:393`). The gap was closed by **`029_household_liveness.sql`** on 2026-07-31; re-verified by `pg_get_functiondef` on both databases, byte-identical. **Part 4 had no open blocker**, and `032` shipped against exactly this guarantee. **Also open:** `household_invites` policies never inspected (gates Part 1's pre-flight lookup); Parts 1–3 confirmed against **prod only**, dev unverified (reconciliation queries in the spec). Parts 1+2 are coordinated SQL + `useProvisions.js` changes, not pure migrations. Migration numbers assigned at point-of-build. | `docs/specs/built/SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md` |

> **⚠️ Sequencing:** this **outranks the 025/026 prod gate and 027**. Two of the five defects allow action with no account at all. Also carried: **the disclosure decision is OPEN** — the spec's original "not warranted" rested on a `reltuples` estimate of "2 households / 3 members"; counted directly, prod is **21 users / 23 live households / 19 people with a live membership**. Dan's call, to be made against the real numbers.

> **✅ 2026-08-03 — Part 1 (`030`) SHIPPED to PROD via RELEASE-2026-08.** `join_household(uuid)` dropped for `join_household(p_invite_code text)`; `invites_select` re-scoped `qual=true`→`is_member_of(household_id)`; `anon` EXECUTE + table grants revoked. Applied to the prod DB, then `dev→main` fast-forward merge + push (`8cbbf43`). Verified on prod: single `join_household(text)` row, no anon in ACL, `invites_select` = `is_member_of`; F8 — a member read returns rows, a non-member zero. The `?invite=` path surfaces the three server error strings via a `bootstrap_new_user` fallback (`90d5b92`) — `acceptInvite` was dead code, so `db5ec66` alone never called the RPC. **Remaining: Parts 2, 3, 4b, 5** + the five unpinned `search_path` functions.
>
> **✅ 2026-07-31 — SHIPPED to dev AND prod:** **Part 4a** (`028` — revoke `anon` DML on `known_stores`, `provision_cycles`, `shopping_sessions`) and **Part 0** (`029` — household + account liveness in `is_member_of` + `bootstrap_new_user`). 4a verified by anon-key REST going `206 Content-Range: 0-0/56` → `401 / 42501 permission denied`. 0 verified by calling the real function with an injected JWT subject: `Test House 200` (deleted 2026-07-10) `true → false`, three live households unchanged. Both `CREATE OR REPLACE`d in place, OIDs unchanged (dev 18480 / prod 43112).
>
> **⚠️ THE SPEC IS NOW WRONG ABOUT ORDER.** `docs/specs/built/SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md` still argues 1→2→3→4. Measurement inverted it — severity ranks by **credential requirement first**, regression risk second. A future session reading that spec cold will build the wrong part first. **Correct the spec before drafting Part 1.**

**Part 1 — `join_household` validates the invite server-side.** `join_household(uuid)` takes a bare household id and performs **no** invite check, **no** membership check and **no** liveness check — an authenticated caller can grant themselves membership in any household by UUID. **Done when:** `join_household(uuid)` is gone; its replacement validates the invite server-side **including `households.deleted_at`** (per 029's precedent — otherwise it is an unguarded second door into a dead household); `anon` EXECUTE revoked; the client-side pre-flight lookup deleted with three distinct server-raised error messages replacing it; `household_invites` SELECT locked to members; invite-accept and rejoin-after-leave both verified on the deployed dev preview.

> **⚠️ INSPECT BEFORE DRAFTING PART 1:** `household_invites` RLS policies have **never been looked at**. If a permissive SELECT policy exists, **every live invite code in the system is readable with the bundled anon key** — a zero-account path into any household, which would outrank Part 1 itself.

### Ladder event taxonomy — the R1–R5 activation events every app fires identically *(NEW 2026-08-05 — Velayo OS scope; gates the Funnel Inspector fleet-wide)*

| Priority | Feature | Spec |
|---|---|---|
| **P0 — NOW (Velayo OS)** | **Define the closed, named set of activation-ladder events (R1–R5) and declare each event's source.** The Funnel Inspector is chartered in `docs/AGENTS.md` but cannot be staffed without it — and divergent per-app event names defeat the whole fleet-charter model, so **this is the last cheap moment to get it right**. Governing rule already decided: **behaviour comes from telemetry, outcomes come from the database** — Splunk RUM + DEM custom telemetry for behaviour, Supabase for outcomes. The **R2 invite pivot** (the depth/thesis metric) is a `household_members` fact and must be assigned to the **database**, never computed from RUM (telemetry is sampled, best-effort, blockable; on disagreement the database wins and the telemetry gap is filed as a defect). Second half of the session: audit what Splunk RUM/DEM **already captures** versus what needs new instrumentation, and write the gap down. **Forward note (from the 2026-08-05 handoff, parked here so it isn't lost):** the event set becomes an **app-architecture** concern the moment it is instrumented — custom telemetry event names fired from `App.js`/`useProvisions.js` are a cross-cutting client pattern, and the R2 pivot's authoritative source is `household_members` + the invite seam. Expect an `ARCHITECTURE.md` entry in this repo **at build time**, not before. **Done when:** a named event list exists with each event's source declared (telemetry vs. database), R2 is explicitly assigned to the database, and the already-captured-vs-needs-building gap is written down. | `docs/AGENTS.md` (charter); event spec unwritten |

The grant is therefore the *only* gate, and it is open. An `anon`-key caller who knows or guesses a `household_id` can write rows onto that household's shared list, **with no account**. **This is PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by `026`** — `CREATE OR REPLACE` preserves the existing ACL, and `026` changes only cycle resolution in the body. Promoting `026` was still correct: holding it back would have kept prod on the unfixed insert path while fixing nothing here. **Why it leaves the LATER bucket:** that item is explicitly *"a capability-surface audit, not an assumed breach"*, on the reasoning that most of the 21 "take a `household_id` and rely on internal checks rather than the grant." **`insert_list_item` has no internal check** — it is the case that reasoning does not cover, and it is write-capable rather than read-only. Same severity class as Parts 1 and 2: **no credential required.** **Done when:** either `anon` EXECUTE is revoked (matching the `add_meal_to_list` precedent from 2026-07-29) **or** an `is_member_of(p_household_id)` guard is added and the anon call proven to return `42501` from outside the SQL editor — decide which, do not do neither. Live `list_items` still pointing at that cycle are never swept to the new cycle or to NULL, **on either branch**. Every close that leaves items unrolled strands them. **Evidence (prod, 2026-08-17): 16 live rows across BVI (8), Sacandaga (7), Madbury (1), newest 2026-08-08** — actively firing, not residue. **NOT fixed by `026`** (that closes the *insert* side) and **not fixed by `038`** (that stops double-*open* cycles). **The 16 rows are deliberately LEFT IN PLACE as live evidence** — do not repoint them before the fix, or the next close re-dirties them and the trail is gone. **Done when:** `close_cycle` sweeps unrolled live survivors on both branches (repoint or NULL — decide which; NULL is a legitimate post-`026` state); a fresh close with unrolled items produces **zero** new stranded rows on dev; and only **then** the 16 prod rows are repaired in one one-off keyed by UUID. **Not demo-blocking** — `get_list_items_for_household` never reads `cycle_id`, so no user sees any of this. Not in `SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md`'s scope; needs its own scoped item. **The fix pattern is already proven:** derive from `auth.jwt()->>'sub'`, drop the parameter, revoke `anon` — shipped as an additive migration → client deploy → removal migration (`useProvisions.js:1559` is the call site). **Done when:** no overload accepts `p_clerk_id`, `anon` EXECUTE is revoked, and the forged shape returns `PGRST202` verified from outside the SQL editor. |

These are 400/404 conditions, not server errors — so **every bad invite attempt logs as a server error in Splunk**, muddying real-error alerting. Fix: raise with a SQLSTATE PostgREST maps to 4xx, or catch + return a structured 4xx. Migration change, so it did NOT gate RELEASE-2026-08; the client already surfaces the message strings correctly (`90d5b92`). |

| — | **The list RPC still reads the DORMANT `catalog_items.is_staple`, overwriting per-household staple state on every poll** *(NEW 2026-08-10; found while diagnosing an inert Staples filter — **its own defect, deliberately NOT fixed inside the rail work**)* | Migration `016` moved staple status to **row-presence in `household_staples`** and states plainly that `catalog_items.is_staple` is left in place only for rollback and that **no read path uses it after this migration**. One does. `get_list_items_for_household` selects `ci.is_staple` in its join (`000_canonical_baseline.sql:780`), and `useProvisions.js:118` merges that value straight into `catalogMap` — **clobbering the correct `stapleSet.has(item.id)` stamping** from `fetchStapleSet`. **Blast radius is the shared list, not just Browse:** every item on the list gets its staple state re-derived from the shared global column on each poll cycle, so a household's own staple choice can silently revert — the exact failure mode `016` was written to end (its "Symptom 1"). Fix is a coordinated RPC + client change (drop `ci.is_staple` from the join, stop merging it), so it needs its own commit and its own dev verification. **Note the client already stamps correctly in two other places** (`useProvisions.js` 584/1418) — this is one inconsistent path, not a broken model. |

The design changed materially in the hand: **Clear chip cut**, **descriptor verb settled on `Showing only` for every phase**, **pagers built then replaced by a styled scrollbar**, **first-run nudge built then cut**. Spec moved to `docs/specs/built/`. See DONE.)* | — |
| — | **Staples descriptor phrasing — a predicate is joined into a category list as a peer** *(NEW 2026-08-10; carved out of the rail build, deliberately not fixed there)* | `Showing only Staples, Produce and 1 more` lists Staples alongside categories as though it were one, but Staples is a **predicate that intersects** the others (`household_staples` row-presence, migration 016 — Layer 1), not a third category set. The line therefore claims three category sets when the truth is *stapled items within two categories*. **Grammatically fine, semantically wrong** — an intersection rendered as a union. The count is correct; only the phrasing lies. Needs its own copy pattern rather than a wider name list. |
| — | **`Multiple GoTrueClient instances detected` in the prod and dev console** *(NEW 2026-08-10; observed during the rail build — **promoted from LATER**, see "Consolidate multiple Supabase client instances")* | More than one Supabase client is constructed in the same browser context, sharing one auth storage key. **Non-fatal but undefined behaviour** under concurrent token refresh. Promoted out of LATER because it sits directly in front of the **Clerk auth-plane flip** — an auth-plane change is the worst moment to be carrying two clients racing on one storage key. Root cause already diagnosed in the LATER row: `useProvisions.js` and `ActiveHouseholdContext.js` each create a client. Confirmed SEPARATE from the 2026-06-29 name-change hang. Design session before touching — the blast radius is the whole auth/token flow. |
| — | **Category data cleanup — merge duplicate bakery categories, retire `Mexican Asian`** *(NEW 2026-08-09; sequenced STRICTLY after the Clerk cutover)* | Merge `Bakery & Bread` / `Bread & Desserts`; retire `Mexican Asian`. Needs its own spec — this is live prod catalog data, and `catalog_items.category` is an **open set** with no enum constraint, so a merge is a data migration with user-visible consequences. **Must not land during the Clerk cutover.** Note the rail's 📦 fallback is what keeps unknown values rendering correctly in the meantime, so this cleanup is quality work, not a rail dependency. |
| — | **Sticky category rail beneath the search bar** *(NEW 2026-08-09; deliberately cut from v1)* | Deferred as its own change **after** the rail ships and verifies. Interacts with the photo-header scroll behaviour and the phase-1 hidden state, so it is a separate problem from the rail itself. |
| — | **Audit the Drive `Velayo OS` folder against the repo; retire or archive** *(NEW 2026-08-09)* | A parallel doc set (`SESSION_LOG.md`, `ROADMAP.md` ×2, `CLAUDE_OS.md`, `VELAYO_BRIEF.md`) sits in Google Drive alongside the repo. **Two files literally named `ROADMAP.md` in one folder** is evidence it has already drifted. The repo is the source of truth; decide retire-or-archive per file rather than leaving a second plausible canon. |
| — | **Vercel + Bitwarden recovery codes into the vault; Bitwarden's own recovery onto a physical card** *(NEW 2026-08-09; caused a live lockout)* | Vercel recovery codes were not stored — that gap produced tonight's lockout. Bitwarden's own recovery material **cannot live inside the vault it opens**, so it goes on the physical card alongside the master password. Pairs with the two-YubiKey decision (one on the keyring, one in the lake safe). |
| — | **Migrate the dev Supabase project off legacy JWT anon keys to `sb_publishable_`** *(NEW 2026-08-09)* | Prod (`parpauldmbetptkmdwbd`) is already on the new `sb_publishable_` format; dev (`zxwtxjjmssykhqrghouf`) is still on legacy `eyJ…` JWT anon keys. Get both environments onto one generation so "the anon key" means one thing. Note the related standing watch-item: **migrate off Supabase legacy anon/service_role keys** (LATER) — the Synthetics `apikey` header must move in lockstep. |
| — | **"Fabric Softemer" orphan row cleanup** | Free-typed list row has no `catalog_item_id`. Rename or delete it on prod so it doesn't surface as a broken state for other members. |
| — | **Roll-forward orphan guard** | Decide whether `close_cycle` should block (or skip) items with no `catalog_item_id` to stop new orphans accumulating across cycles. |
| — | **Decommission "Reset Public Schema Permissions" query** | This saved query stripped all `authenticated`/`anon` grants on the dev sandbox (Jun 16 incident). Rename to "⚠️ DANGER — strips all grants" or delete from both dev and prod SQL editors. |
| — | **Add "commit + push after edits" directive to CLAUDE.md** | Stranded commit at session start caused a Vercel-stale-build false alarm. Add explicit rule: after any code edit, commit + push before closing the session. |
| 4 | **Cascade soft-delete (catalog → list)** | Deleting a custom catalog item should cascade to active `list_items` rows. Same gesture as the Delete half of #2 — pairs with it. |
| 5 | **Fix close_cycle contributor carry-forward** | When rolling items forward, copy `list_item_contributors` rows to new `list_items`. Currently badges reset to `added_by` only after wrap-up. Fix is in the `close_cycle` RPC. |
| 6 | **Re-enable RLS on provision_cycles, shopping_sessions, known_stores** | Confirmed disabled in prod (matches dev). Anon key can cross-household read/write these tables. Low stakes now; address during migration rewrite. |
| 8 | **Global category rename** | `household_category_overrides` table. Lets a household rename "Pantry" → "Dry Goods" etc. Migration pending. |
| 9 | **Reset Household (nuclear option)** | Confirmation-gated. Returns catalog to factory seed and clears household customizations. NOT the everyday undo for hides/deletes — recovery-from-chaos only. |
| 10 | **Replace remaining `window.location.reload()` calls** | Audit codebase; replace all with `refreshCatalog()` pattern. |
| — | **Avatar two-letter monograms (small UX)** | Single-initial contributor badges are ambiguous — two users can both render "D" (Dan Holmes / Dan Test User). Fix: two-letter monogram (DH / DT — names already provide the data). Alternatively: per-user distinct colors, or lean on the existing hover-name. Two-letter likely cleanest. |
| 11 | **Email receipt parser** | Most actionable near-term price ingestion path. No partnerships required. Parse forwarded grocery receipts via email. |

## LATER — Phase 1.5: Active Household Identity

| Feature | Notes |

| **Empty-list state design (starfish parable candidate home)** *(NEW 2026-07-23; from the splash session)* | The starfish parable was considered for the splash and rejected — a threshold can't give it reading time. Its candidate home is the **empty-list state**, which currently has room and nothing in it. Design the empty state as a moment with meaning (the parable, or the harbour/refuge voice) rather than a blank. Pairs with the Trip Complete work (the empty state is *also* where a cleared trip lands). Content beat, not urgent — earns a proper pass when the empty state is next touched. |
| **Phase II shared active-household lens at the harbour/identity layer** | Once the Harbour / `velayo-os` identity layer exists, the "which household is active" signal should be a cross-app primitive shared by all Velayo apps (OurChef, OurManifest, etc.) — not re-implemented per app. Phase I outer-chrome indicator is the OurProvisions-only stopgap; Phase II retires it in favor of the shared lens. Scope belongs to a `velayo-os` session once app #2 starts. |
| **"Your data is yours" editable profile surface** *(banked future beat — 2026-07-09; earns a SPEC when built)* | A "we heard you" beat, not now. Onboarding data belongs to the user: it should live under their name in-app, be editable, be labelled with *why* it exists, on an honest "keep sharing and it gets smarter" loop — Velayo's data posture made visible. Technical shape when built: a **separate editable profile table**, user-owned and mutable (RLS scoped to the user), **seeded from `beta_signups` but never conflated with it** — `beta_signups` stays the immutable, timestamped intake record (founder-only `status`/`fit_note` intact). Do NOT make the intake row user-editable. Small schema decision with a real "why" + verification need → write a proper SPEC when the beat comes up. See ARCHITECTURE "Banked design principle — Your data is yours". |

Clarify with Aidan before speccing; likely rides the editable-profile / Preferences beat. |
| **Invite-arrival expectation-setting** *(→ PROMOTED to NEXT 2026-07-14; re-diagnosed 2026-07-16)* | Set the arriving invitee's expectations on first open: "this is the app; add it to your home screen." Cheap and funnel-critical (protects the depth/second-invite metric). Pairs with the existing PWA-install coach-mark + cold-start specs. Now split across the NEXT items (front-door copy → NOW; referral primitive; Chris query). |
| **Android link-format repro (schemeless vs `https://`)** *(NEW 2026-07-16; 5 min, non-blocking)* | Cheap open test that would settle Heddi's unexplained failure: text an Android phone `ourprovisions.velayo.ai` vs `https://ourprovisions.velayo.ai` and tap both. Leading hypothesis is that Dan's hand-sent link was schemeless where the in-app invite carries `https://`. The referral primitive removes this defect surface *by construction* (app owns the artifact) whether or not the repro is ever run. |

One asset swap, not a redesign — the landing hero and app header are the same scrim primitive at different scales. |
| **Event as a second provisioning context** *(named strategic concept — beta session 2026-07-14; ~Phase 4, own design session)* | Household = standing/permanent; event = bounded/ad-hoc/deadline-driven (golf trip, bachelorette). May express the CI thesis *more* vividly than the household (acute pain, unavoidable collaboration, natural referral surface, easier on-ramp). **Modeling fork (unresolved):** first-class entity with its own lifecycle (planning→active→closed) and a **"claim" / "I'm bringing this"** verb that *divides* responsibility rather than sharing one pot — OR simply a household with an expiry/archive + a flavor. Lean: cheapest first (event = temporary household), validate before promoting. Note the loop: "claim" and priority-signaling both answer *who owns getting this item*, so the event verb may inform the household priority model. No change to Phases 1–3. |

## LATER — Velayo OS / Infrastructure

| Feature | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Splunk Observability Cloud MCP connector** — *extra credit, for Vegas travel downtime* *(NEW 2026-08-18 — Velayo OS / tooling; explicitly NOT this week's scope)* | Covers what is **actually** instrumented — RUM + Synthetics — via Observability Cloud's narrower **MCP Gateway**, which is closer in setup shape to the existing Supabase MCP than the general platform connector. **The general Splunk platform MCP was REJECTED, not deferred:** it needs admin-provisioned enterprise OAuth (heavier than this scale warrants) and carries a **community-reported result-fabrication risk on `tstats` searches** — disqualifying for a tool whose entire job is reporting truth about production. ⚠️ **Realm check FIRST — GCP and GovCloud realms are unsupported;** confirm before spending time on setup. **Done when:** the connector is live, scoped as narrowly as its OAuth flow permits (the Vercel precedent: if read-only and read-write are inseparable, scope by project/realm instead), and a RUM query returns a result cross-checked against the Observability Cloud UI — **not trusted on its own**, given the fabrication concern that killed the platform connector. **Not demo-blocking, not build-blocking.** |
| **Audit the remaining `anon`-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions on prod** *(NEW 2026-08-17 — OurProvisions security; needs a sweep, not spot-fixes; **21 → 20 open after the 2026-08-18 carve-out**)* | After the authorization series closed, **21 SECURITY DEFINER functions remain executable by `anon`** on prod (`authenticated`: 23). The list includes **`delete_household`, `remove_member`, `leave_household`, `insert_list_item`, `delete_custom_catalog_item`** — destructive operations reachable with the bundled anon key and no account. Most take a `household_id` and rely on internal checks rather than the grant, so **this is a capability-surface audit, not an assumed breach**: each needs a decision of *revoke `anon`* vs *keep and prove the internal guard*. Baseline measured 2026-08-17 via `has_function_privilege` (resolves PUBLIC grants and role inheritance, unlike a raw `proacl` read — the `028` lesson). **Do this as one pass**; fixing them individually as each surfaces is how the class stays open for months. **TWO are carved out into their own NEXT items:** `create_household` (known, already-proven fix) and — **added 2026-08-18** — **`insert_list_item`**, which was checked individually while promoting `026` and turned out to have **no internal guard at all**, making it the case this item's "relies on internal checks" reasoning explicitly does not cover. ⚠️ **That is a warning about this item's own framing:** the "audit, not a breach" premise is an *assumption* about the other 20, not a measurement of them. **The sweep must verify each body for a membership guard — `position('is_member_of' in pg_get_functiondef(oid))` — not infer one from the fact that a `household_id` parameter exists.** |
| **Get write access for migrations, or accept manual apply as the standing process** *(NEW 2026-08-17)* | The Supabase MCP connects as `supabase_read_only_user` in **both** environments and exposes no `apply_migration`, so all four of tonight's migrations were applied by hand in the SQL editor across two environments. It worked, but it makes every migration session a manual relay and puts the apply step outside any verification the assistant can perform. Decide deliberately: provision a write-capable path, or record manual-apply as the intended process so it stops being re-discovered mid-session. |
| **Content Loop role charters** *(NEW 2026-08-15 — Velayo OS / Growth)* | **Content_Idea, Editor and Publisher have no charter blocks** in `docs/AGENTS.md` — no trigger, steps, output format or verification, which is the format every existing charter uses. The Content Loop is designed and recorded in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, but the roster does not yet carry it. **Content_Idea's charter matters most:** it is the fleet's nearest true Agent, missing only the autonomous trigger, and per the standalone-watcher constraint it must be built with its own read access — never as a step inside SESSION END's script — or promotion becomes a rewrite instead of a config change. |
| **CONTENT_IDEA step-by-step trigger routine** *(NEW 2026-08-15 — Velayo OS)* | Written in the same shape as SESSION START / SESSION END / BUILD, and paired to this project's SESSION END (the only moment its source docs change). Discussed as buildable, not written. Pairs with the charter row above. |
| **Stale "promotes when" lines across the roster** *(NEW 2026-08-15 — Velayo OS)* | Scribe's and Tester's promotion criteria both describe **removing the human**, which under the 2026-08-15 ladder yields a *scheduled Seat*, not an Agent. They need rewriting against all three conditions, not just trigger. Cheap; purely a docs correction, but leaving them invites a false promotion later. |
| **Three Growth charters name no trigger** *(NEW 2026-08-15 — Velayo OS / Growth)* | Funnel Inspector, Feedback Scribe and Copy Drafter predate the new ladder and none specifies a trigger, so none can be tiered. **Funnel Inspector's judgment is also disputed** — classifying users against a fixed, closed taxonomy may be a predetermined sequence rather than judgment. Resolve alongside the ladder event taxonomy row below, which already gates that role. |
| **Feedback Scribe tagging taxonomy** *(NEW 2026-08-05 — Velayo OS / Growth)* | The Feedback Scribe is chartered in `docs/AGENTS.md` but has no tag vocabulary, so it cannot classify anything deterministically. Note the Growth group's governing rule applies: **no Growth agent addresses a human unsupervised — draft, never send.** Also deliberately deferred alongside it: **do not automate the founder conversations at beta scale** — at n=10, personally answering users is the highest-signal research available. |
| **Business Foundation lane charters** *(NEW 2026-08-05 — Velayo OS)* | The Harbour's fourth lane has **zero agents and zero charters**. Every other lane now has at least a chartered roster in `docs/AGENTS.md`. Not urgent, but it is the visible hole in the crew model. |
| **Per-app agent instance table in `docs/AGENTS.md`** *(NEW 2026-08-05 — Velayo OS; trigger = app #2, not a date)* | Today the roster is prose referencing OurProvisions-specific artifacts. **Charters are fleet-level; instances are app-level** — Scribe/Inspector/Watchman are structural and shared, while Tester/Fixer/Analyst are instanced per app because "correct" is app-specific. Deliberately not built early; add the instance table when a second app makes the distinction load-bearing. Same trigger class as "Split company log into `velayo-os/docs`." |
| **Invite code namespace is permanently consumed** *(NEW 2026-07-31; found during Part 1 verification — NOT a Part 1 item)* | `household_invites_code_key` is a **global** unique index on `code`, not partial — so a soft-deleted invite's code can **never be reused**, by regeneration or by another household. `createInvite` (`useProvisions.js:1077`) generates codes as `Math.random().toString(36).substring(2,8).toUpperCase()` — 6 chars, non-cryptographic, **with no collision retry**: on a duplicate the insert simply fails and the user sees an error. 36⁶ ≈ 2.2bn, but the practical limit is birthday-bounded and every code ever issued is spent forever. Prod holds 74 rows today so this is not near-term, but it degrades silently and the failure mode is "creating an invite errors for no visible reason." Fix is a retry loop plus a wider or crypto-random code — or making uniqueness partial on `deleted_at is null`, which would need care given codes are shared into chat apps. **Do not touch the existing index as part of an exploit fix.** |
| **Retention policy — soft-delete window, then purge** *(NEW 2026-07-31; supersedes "one-time test-data purge")* | A one-time deletion solves the backlog once; a retention window (soft-deleted rows recoverable for N days, then purged) solves it permanently **and** clears the backlog on first run. Fleet-level schema pattern, not an OurProvisions decision — same class as `externalId = internal users.id`. **Blockers:** prod has no backups; every FK referencing `catalog_items` is `NO ACTION`, so purge order must be deliberate; and Test House 200's orphaned membership was the **only** row exercising the `029` defect — deleting test data destroys the evidence that a fix works. Deleting test *users* is separately hazardous: `users.email`/`clerk_id` are unique-not-null and `bootstrap_new_user` upserts on conflict. |
| **Harbour Identity layer + fleet payment system** *(strategic direction; NEW 2026-07-27 — Platform/Harbour scope, own design session)* | One Velayo identity spanning every vessel — the seam a **fleet-level billing system** attaches to (one human = one billing relationship across apps). Stable identity seam first, billing policy iterates on top. Enabled by the **`externalId = internal user id` fleet standard** (DECISIONS 2026-07-27): new vessels adopt it from provisioning day; OurProvisions' externalId migration (NEXT) is the retrofit pilot. Needs a dedicated session: identity model (shared Harbour user vs per-vessel), the billing relationship shape, and how RLS/`sub` claims resolve across apps. |
| **About page / founder's letter** *(brand/content; NEW 2026-07-21)* | The 2026-07-21 session generated good long-form writing with no current home — the full **starfish parable**, the long-form **two-part philosophy** (world / your peace), and the **harbour-as-refuge** principle ("safe but never stuck"). Home it as an About page / founder's letter; **pair with the `ourprovisions.app` story surface**. Content mostly exists; the decision is *where it lives*, not *whether to write it*. |
| **Splunk OTel Collector on AWS Lightsail** *(deferred to Phase 3–4; NEW 2026-07-15)* | Server-side DB/infra telemetry (would have shown a healthy Supabase during the 07-15 outage — real value, but not the bottleneck). Splunk's free 15-host tier removes the cost objection, not the attention cost: a VM to patch, a Compose file, a scrape config that fails silently. **Architecture design is sound — keep it for when it's *wanted*, not *needed*.** RUM + Synthetics already deliver ~80% of the benefit with zero operational surface. Deferred deliberately: "a fun afternoon, not a fire drill." |
| **Migrate off Supabase legacy anon/service_role keys** *(watch-item; NEW 2026-07-15)* | Legacy keys are on a deprecation path toward Publishable/Secret keys. Not urgent — the app uses the legacy anon key and the Synthetics uptime check matches it (so both move together). Revisit when Supabase forces it; update the Synthetics `apikey` header in lockstep. |
| **Consolidate multiple Supabase client instances to one shared client** *(design-first; **PROMOTED TO NEXT 2026-08-10** — no longer "non-urgent", it sits in front of the Clerk auth-plane flip. This row is retained for its diagnosis; the live item is in NEXT.)* | `useProvisions.js` and `ActiveHouseholdContext.js` each create a Supabase client sharing one auth storage key → persistent GoTrueClient multiple-instance warning. Confirmed SEPARATE from the name-change hang (2026-06-29). ~~Non-urgent~~; auth/token-flow blast radius means a design session before touching. |
| **Required first/last name at signup** | Clerk config + backfill + social-auth check. Reduces email-prefix display fallbacks; decided 2026-06-29 this makes the rare unnamed-member case a non-issue rather than something to engineer around. |
| **Show which household an invite link is for, in the Share panel** *(ExD polish)* | "Invite to <name>" in the Share panel — makes the household↔link binding legible and prevents stale-link confusion structurally (complements the 2026-06-29 stale-link reset). |
| **Authenticate/link the `vercel` CLI for Claude Code** | So deploy status can be verified directly instead of gating on a human dashboard check. Surfaced 2026-06-29 when promotion couldn't confirm prod-build-green programmatically (no `gh`, `vercel` CLI not authed/linked). |
| Promote "Crew only" to a reusable Access Group | When a 2nd internal app or 4th person appears — scales without per-hire edits. |
| Split company log into `velayo-os/docs` | Trigger = app #2's first session, not a date. Filter-based split, not a migration (scope tags already in place). |
| Sync on unfocused tabs | Browser `setInterval` throttles to ~1 min on backgrounded tabs, causing perceived lag. Consider `visibilitychange` refresh-on-focus, or Supabase Realtime if Clerk/Realtime auth incompatibility is ever resolved. |
| Invite-accept creates duplicate memberships | Accepting an invite adds a membership without retiring the user's auto-bootstrapped household. Should move-or-block until multi-household is real. |
| Apply migrations 001 + 002 to prod | Required before multi-household ships to real users. Currently dev-only. |
| **Location-confirm first-landing** *(gated behind store-awareness arc)* | When location is shared and a confident GPS match exists, offer a one-tap confirm ("You're in Day, NY — shopping for NewLeaf?") — never a silent switch. Location gets a voice, never a vote. |
| Remove temp `[ActiveHousehold TEST]` console log from `App.js` | Added for spine verification; remove before multi-household UI ships. |
| Audit SECURITY DEFINER functions for `set search_path` | `get_my_households()` and `get_household_id_for_current_user()` lack explicit `set search_path`. Low risk now; address when consolidating helper functions. |
| `bootstrap_new_user` step-1 dead-logic cleanup | `if v_user_id is null` guard is always true because the INSERT above never sets `v_user_id` (no RETURNING clause). Harmless but misleading. Fix when rewriting bootstrap as the real active-household fix. |
| Reconcile git remotes across machines | Canonical remote is `Velayo-ai/ourprovisions`; stale Surface clone pointed at `dan-velayo/ourprovisions`. Reconcile when doing next per-machine setup. |
| Local Supabase (CLI/Docker) for offline dev | Seeded from `000_canonical_baseline.sql` — for genuinely-offline boat development. Only if offline dev becomes a real need. |

## LATER — Catalog / View Refinements

| Feature | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Retire the redundant `uq_live_list_item` partial index** *(NEW 2026-07-29)* | `list_items` carries BOTH a total unique index on `(household_id, catalog_item_id)` and the partial `uq_live_list_item` (migration 020) on the same columns. The total index strictly subsumes the partial (at most one row per pair, ever — tombstones are reused via resurrect, not accumulated), so the partial costs a write-time index and buys nothing. Worse, its name documents a **multi-tombstone model we don't implement** — that misreading cost a full round-trip this session (the ON-CONFLICT-infers-which-index question). Drop it once confirmed no code/`ON CONFLICT` names it explicitly. |
| Multi-item / category-level Hide | Hide several items (or a whole category) in one action. Build ONLY if user feedback shows one-by-one hiding is a real pain. Deferred by decision June 8. |
| **Shop filter-pill bar (who-added, per-store)** *(horizon — surfaces when stores are introduced)* | Shop gains its own filter-pill bar separate from the declutter cycle: filter-by-who-added (Elly/Helen/DH) and per-store filtering (split a trip across stores; hide the other store's items). The cycle handles the *view* axis (grouped/flat + hide-noise); pills handle the *content* axis (what's shown). Captured in `docs/SPEC_declutter_cycle.md` future section. |

Shopping intelligence layer. Captures behavior silently and learns from it.

| Store detection | GPS match against `known_stores`. Receipt scan confirms. Manual fallback only for new stores. |
| Smart list ordering | Sort list to match user's natural path through their store. Per-user, per-store. |

## LATER — Phase 3: Price Intelligence

The intelligence layer. Prices are infrastructure, not UI. Everything feeds from receipt scanning.

| Feature | Notes |
|---|---|
| Receipt scan (camera) | Capture receipt image → Claude parses → populates price_history |
| Email receipt parser | Forward grocery email receipts → parse → price_history |
| Price history | Per item, per store, over time |
| Cross-store comparison | "Eggs are $1.20 cheaper at Market Basket" |
| DB: `receipts` | `id, household_id, scanned_by, store_id, raw_image_url, parsed_json, total, purchased_at` |
| DB: `price_history` | `id, household_id, catalog_item_id, store_id, price, quantity, scanned_at, receipt_id` |
| Strategic partnerships | Fetch, Ibotta (receipt aggregators); Market Basket, Hannaford, Whole Foods; Apple Wallet, Gmail |

| **OurDiscovery** | Family experiences layer |
| **OurHelper** | Neighborhood mutual aid — share lists with a helper |
| **Data Marketplace** | Opt-in anonymized household behavior data. One consent field unlocks the future. |
| **Child mode** | Visual picture-based interface for kids; requests land on parent list |

---

## DECISIONS LOG

| Date | Decision |
|---|---|
| 2026-08-30 | **Solo-start and earned-Our were promoted to prod as ONE release, not staggered.** The solo-start spec names the wordmark a *sibling, not a dependency* — either could have shipped alone. They shipped together because the narrative only reads correctly as a pair: a header claiming "Our" above a sheet asking you to name your *first* place is the same contradiction the 2026-08-24 incident was made of. Sequencing inside the release was NOT optional though — `042` to prod first, then `dev→main`, then live verification. |
| 2026-08-30 | **Earned-Our: spec intent beat spec wording, and the deviation was recorded rather than silently made.** The spec said "opacity fade (no layout shift)"; taken literally, a phantom-width invisible "Our" leaves "Provisions" permanently off-centre in a `text-align: center` band — worst in the solo case the fix exists for. Shipped as `opacity` + `max-width` together. **The rule this sets: when a spec's literal instruction defeats its stated purpose, follow the purpose and write down that you did.** A silent deviation is indistinguishable from a bug six weeks later. |
| 2026-08-30 | **ROADMAP moves: solo-start VERIFY → DONE, earned-Our ACTIVE → DONE.** Solo-start was double-verified — the 8/26 full pass (bidirectional D11, live Prem/Cyrus regression re-run) plus an independent 8/30 DB re-confirmation of items 4/6/7, results matching. Earned-Our was browser-verified across all four cases on dev, then live-verified on prod. |
| 2026-08-30 | **Flagged, no process change yet: two Claude Code sessions on separate machines ran the same catch-up independently, unaware of each other,** producing divergent local commits that needed a manual 3-way doc merge. The cheap fix is probably "always `git pull` at session start, on every machine" — but it is recorded as an open discipline question rather than adopted silently, because the merge also *worked*: the conflict surfaced the disagreement instead of one machine silently overwriting the other. |
| 2026-08-30 | **Fetch before concluding a document is missing — a stale remote-tracking ref manufactures false history.** The 2026-08-29 catch-up read the tree honestly and still got the headline wrong: it recorded "all 10 verification items UNRUN" while `b5f0d33`, four days old on `origin/dev`, already recorded all 10 as passed. Nothing was wrong with the reasoning; the input was stale. **`git fetch` is part of reading the repo, not a step before pushing** — the same lesson the 2026-08-25 meal-spec recovery paid for, now paid again in the opposite direction: concluding work was *undone* rather than a document *lost*. |
| 2026-08-29 | **A build session that skipped SESSION END is reconstructed from commits, never from recollection.** The 2026-08-25/26 solo-start build left the log claiming "Build not started" for four days. The catch-up entry was authored by reading `42afe32..70868e7` and the tree, and its deployment claims were settled by **fetching the dev and prod bundles and grepping them** — dev carries `just_signed_up` / `pending_ref_code` / `discard_unclaimed_household` / `p_ref_code`, prod carries none. The stale entry itself stays untouched (append-only); only ROADMAP and ARCHITECTURE, which describe *current* state, are corrected in place. |
| 2026-08-29 | **Built ≠ done: a feature stays in `active/` with no DONE stamp until its verification items have actually been run.** ⚠️ **The rule stands; its 2026-08-29 application was wrong** — solo-start’s 10 items had in fact been run on 2026-08-26 (`b5f0d33`), unseen behind a stale remote, so the DONE stamp withheld here was already earned. **Presence in a bundle is still not behaviour**, and items 4 (the D11 control case) and 6 (no invite row) remain settleable only against the database — which is exactly how they were settled, twice. Precedent deliberately not followed: earlier DEV-ONLY features were stamped DONE *after* being walked on dev, not before. |
| 2026-08-26 | **Solo-start is verified on DEV, and that is deliberately not the same as done.** All 10 spec items pass plus the originating incident as a regression check, so the feature is **DONE (dev)** and its spec moves to `built/` — but `main` carries none of it and `042` is dev-only. **The prod gate stays closed until explicitly promoted, migration first.** Shipping the client ahead of `042` would PGRST202 every `?ref=` arrival. |
| 2026-08-26 | **A defaulted 4th parameter is added and the 3-arg dropped IN THE SAME TRANSACTION — the drop is what keeps resolution deterministic.** Leaving a 3-arg beside a 4-arg-with-default makes the deployed 3-named-arg call **ambiguous** ("function is not unique") and every sign-in fails. Because the new parameter is defaulted, the currently-deployed client keeps resolving — so `042` needs no 036/037-style two-file split, and there is no window in which the live bundle is broken. ⚠️ Verify this function **by parameter NAMES, not arity**: `(text,text,text,text)` is the same *type* signature `037` dropped, and identity still comes from `auth.jwt()`, never the body. |
| 2026-08-26 | **The D11 "created this session" condition is bounded server-side as a deliberately generous backstop, not as the guard.** "This session" is a client-side fact the server cannot observe, so `discard_unclaimed_household` requires `created_by = caller` plus `created_at > now() - interval '1 hour'`. The window is loose on purpose — conditions 2–4 (sentinel name, zero items, sole member) are what make the delete safe; a tight window would only add flakiness on a slow signup. The function **returns `{discarded, reason}` rather than raising**: a refusal is a normal outcome, and failing the user’s successful join because we declined to tidy up would be the tail wagging the dog. |
| 2026-08-26 | **The referral code is minted at bootstrap in uppercase hex, 8 chars — closing the `023`-vs-D9 reconciliation the design session left open** (D9 left charset and length to the builder). Hex was chosen because it contains **no letter O and no letter I**, so the 0/O and 1/I read-aloud collisions are impossible *by construction* rather than by a hand-maintained exclusion list. ⚠️ Recorded as **hexadecimal**, not "alphanumeric" — the distinction is the entire reason for the choice. The upsert uses `referral_code = coalesce(users.referral_code, excluded.referral_code)`, so a code already held is **never rotated**. July’s trigger-set + separate-generator description in the `023` row is superseded. |
| 2026-08-26 | **The GoTrueClient `storageKey` fix is hygiene and is explicitly NOT a fix for the membership-removal toast.** The two are structurally unrelated: every client sets `persistSession: false`, and request auth rides the per-request Clerk JWT injected by the fetch wrapper — it never touches a GoTrue session, so a storage-key collision cannot change which rows `get_my_households` returns. The toast fires on a **successful** response that omits the active household. Recorded so no future reader mistakes `04512ed` for a root-cause fix. |
| 2026-08-26 | **The membership-removal toast has exactly one trigger, and it is a poll — not realtime.** `checkPresence`, a 30-second `setInterval` over `get_my_households`; App's own delete path passes `notifyRemoval=false` and never reaches the notice. Instrumented rather than fixed (`6891a31`): **`household.row_still_readable`** cross-references `households`' `is_member_of(id)` SELECT policy against the poll result, so a still-readable row proves a false-positive race and an unreadable one proves a real removal. **One boolean settles it on the next recurrence.** |
| 2026-08-26 | **Item 11's clipboard half accepted as untested rather than blocking the session.** No browser lacking `navigator.share` was available. Low risk (a reviewed field split), worth an opportunistic check, not worth holding a verified build for. |
| 2026-08-25 | **`navigator.share` gets the link in its `url` field, never concatenated into `text`; the clipboard fallback keeps it appended.** Platforms that render `url` separately showed the link twice and had no clean target for a preview card. Not a stylistic split — both share functions reuse one `text` variable, so removing the URL from the prose alone would have put a **linkless invitation** on the desktop path behind an "Invite copied" toast: an invite that cannot be accepted. The two branches genuinely need different strings. |
| 2026-08-25 | **Solo-start welcome sheet designed + spec'd (D1–D5).** Fires once for bare-URL signups, gated on a durable `sessionStorage.just_signed_up` flag **∧** the `"My Household"` sentinel — a stray surviving flag on a named place stays silent (*behavior before label, applied to a trigger*). Place vocabulary, **skippable** ("I'll do this later" leaves the sentinel, no re-fire, no nagging), **Continue disabled while empty** (an enabled empty Continue is a second Skip wearing a misleading label). **The naming moment IS the invite-recovery moment** — code entry is an inline expander on the same sheet, never a second screen, so recovery is present at the exact moment of mis-enrollment with no flow to abandon. Routed through the existing server-validated `join_household(text)` (migration `030`): **one join implementation, two entrances.** |
| 2026-08-25 | **Two share verbs (D6–D8).** **Invite aboard** (`?invite=`, minted per share, names the place) vs. **Share the app** (`?ref=`, static, **never** names the place — the copy itself cannot be mistaken for a list invitation). Both delegate to `navigator.share()`; **no confirm interstitial on either** — labels and visual hierarchy carry the distinction, and a confirm punishes the common case (if fat-fingering shows up in the field, fix spacing and styling, not with a dialog). **Hard rule: every in-app share path carries `invite` or `ref`, never a naked URL** — so a bare arrival *means* organic rather than sitting in the ambiguous middle that produced the Prem incident. |
| 2026-08-25 | **Referral carry (D7, D9, D10).** `ref` **attributes but never enrolls** — the recipient lands as a solo-start and still sees the welcome sheet, so the referral tree becomes a database fact with **zero change to the recipient's experience**. One **stable** `referral_code` per user (invite codes stay per-share and expirable — **different species, never unified**); `referred_by` written **once** at arrival, self-referral rejected server-side; **nothing written at send** ("measure arrivals, not sends" — July lock upheld). **Placement half of `SPEC_referral_primitive.md` SUPERSEDED** — the surface is the household sheet beside Invite aboard, not Preferences, because **the verbs only disambiguate as a pair**. July's other two decisions stand unchanged. ⚠️ **Open reconciliation:** the migration `023` row still describes a *trigger-set* code plus a backfill; D9 leaves mint timing and charset to the builder. Settle the two into one at point-of-build. |
| 2026-08-25 | **D11 — naming is claiming.** Code-join from the welcome sheet soft-deletes the machine-created solo place under a **four-condition guard** (created-this-session ∧ sentinel-named ∧ zero items ∧ sole member). Cleanup exists **ONLY at that user-initiated pivot, never as a background process**. One name, one item, or one member makes a place theirs permanently — and text typed but never Continued was never saved, so the guard correctly still sees a sentinel. Generalizes beyond places: **a machine-created resource may be silently cleaned only while provably unclaimed.** |
| 2026-08-24 | **Earned-Our wordmark reinstated; supersedes §8 of the retired `SPEC_splash_vessel_identity_v2.md`.** Header renders "Provisions" for everyone by default — **including while membership loads** (*behaviour before label*: never claim **Our** until the data supports it). "Our" fades in at 2+ members on the active household, drops back on switching to a solo one, driven off `householdMembers.length`. Splash still reads full "OurProvisions" (brand surface) but **the travel/clone hand-off machinery is deleted; the splash simply exits.** Rationale: the mechanic is the Our Concept made visible in-product, and it is **diagnostic** — had it been live, Cyrus's solo session would have self-identified instantly. ⚠️ **Process finding: §8's header change outlived the spec that justified it** — v2 was retired 2026-07-25 when v3 superseded it, but the always-"Our" behaviour it mandated stayed in the code. **Retiring a spec does not revert what it shipped.** |
| 2026-08-24 | **Bare-URL signup is a first-class path, not a fallback.** The Prem/Cyrus incident was **perceptual, not mechanical** — a code-less new user correctly receives their own household. What failed were the *surfaces*: the always-**Our** wordmark and the silent generic `"My Household"`. Fix direction is a first-run naming moment, a "Have an invite code?" entry (which also recovers mangled links), and two deliberate share verbs — **Invite aboard** vs. **Share the app**, carrying a referrer param from day one. |
| 2026-08-24 | **Clerk script CORS-transparency verified on prod — no action, and no SDK bump.** `Sec-Fetch-Mode: cors` + `ACAO: *` confirmed live on **both** redirect hops; clerk-js@5 already sets `crossorigin="anonymous"`. Future "Script error." entries in RUM therefore implicate *other* cross-origin scripts — **Turnstile captcha first suspect**, since it loads only during sign-up, matching the error's timing. Investigate only on recurrence; the Clerk major-version bump is decoupled onto its own schedule. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Catalog sharing between households is optional, per household-pair, via the existing `crew_id` link** — not global, not automatic, and not tied to shared people. Reuses the dormant harbour-crew layer instead of inventing a parallel grouping concept. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Lists remain permanently household-local.** Catalog sharing never implies list sharing — settled explicitly after early conversation conflated the two. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Personal hide needs no change and no household-level counterpart.** `user_hidden_items`' cross-household sync, previously suspected to be a bug, is the correct and desired behaviour (the allergy/dislike case). |
| 2026-08-23 | **Delete becomes two-tier — household-scoped remove vs. delete-everywhere — inside the Edit Item sheet, never on swipe.** Deliberate friction on a rare, consequential action; extends the shipped household-delete (D3) and meals two-stage-confirm patterns rather than adding a third. Swipe stays reserved for personal hide. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Unlinking a crew stops future sync but never strips items already present** — the same principle as a person leaving a household. Removal of access is not removal of what was received. |
| 2026-08-23 | **`insert_custom_catalog_item`'s dedup (018/019) must become crew-aware BEFORE sharing ships**, matching crew-sibling custom items by normalized name — otherwise catalog sharing reopens the duplicate-row bug those migrations closed, at crew scale. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Meals-sharing is deliberately left open.** Do not assume catalog-sharing logic extends to `meals`; the 2026-07-28 give/accept-only lock stands until a session revisits it explicitly. |
| 2026-08-21 | **MEALS ARE ON PROD — the promotion decision open since 2026-08-19 is CLOSED.** Migrations `039` (`quantity_contributed`), `040` (`add_count`), `041` (`decrement_meal_from_list`) applied to prod **in that order**, verified by content (column checks, `pg_get_functiondef`, `proacl`) rather than by a green "success". `dev → main` fast-forwarded twice and confirmed on `ourprovisions.velayo.ai` **by bundle content, never by hash** — Vercel builds prod with different env vars, so hashes legitimately differ. |
| 2026-08-21 | **The meal card adopts `CatalogItemRow`'s Add⇄stepper invariant — "never a stepper at qty 0" — rather than a static Add plus a text badge.** Reuses the pattern every catalog row has followed since 2026-07-10 instead of inventing a second one. The stepper's presence IS the planned signal, so the separate teal "Planned" label was retired the moment it shipped. |
| 2026-08-21 | **`decrement_meal_from_list` is a SECURITY DEFINER RPC, not a client write — forced by a real defect, not preference.** `list_item_meals` has SELECT/INSERT/DELETE policies only, **no UPDATE**, by original `025` design ("a provenance linkage row is delete-and-reinserted, never updated"). The stepper is the first feature that ever needed to amend such a row in place. ⚠️ **The failure mode is the lesson: the client UPDATE matched zero rows and raised NO error** — RLS filters silently — so the list moved correctly while the ledger froze. Half-working with a clean console. **Standing rule reaffirmed: provenance ledgers are server-write-only.** |
| 2026-08-21 | **Un-planning happens through the stepper alone.** The swipe "Remove from list" action was built, tested and then **removed the same session** once the stepper made it redundant — at `add_count === 1` it did the literally identical thing through a less discoverable gesture. Swipe reverted to EDIT-only, matching pre-session behaviour. `removeMealFromList` itself is untouched and still backs `deleteMeal`. |
| 2026-08-21 | **A spec's "currently…" block is evidence, not truth — grep the tree first.** `SPEC_meal_add_count.md` quoted `add_meal_to_list` as ending in `ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING` and directed replacing that clause; `039` had already rewritten it. Building it literally would have dropped the `quantity_contributed` increment and broken every function queued behind it. `040` **extends** rather than replaces, built from `039`'s text. **This is the second time a spec's line numbers or quoted code have drifted from the tree in three sessions.** |
| 2026-08-21 | **Handoff discipline corrected mid-session: anything Cody must build or apply goes in a FILE dropped in `handoff/`, never inline chat text.** Inline pastes did not reach the build session at all — two round trips were lost to it before the rule was set. |
| 2026-08-21 | **Parked, no roadmap slot yet:** the Home tab as a **Harbour-scale concierge** surface (calendar, geofenced Shop-tab default, onboarding — adjacent to the fleet identity-layer thread), and a **"planning hierarchy"** framing (Groceries→Meals→Days→Events→Insights) that **sharpens** `STRATEGY_consumption_signal.md`'s existing tiered-signal thesis rather than replacing it. Neither has design work started. |
| 2026-08-20 | **`deleteMeal` uses SOFT-delete, not hard.** Soft preserves the record of which meal put an item on the list — the loss that ruled hard-delete out — and matches the schema's `deleted_at` contract. **Requires** `fetchMealProvenance` to filter `meals.deleted_at`, done in the same commit; without it a deleted meal resurrects as a phantom badge. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Meal provenance and user contribution are PARALLEL LEDGERS on `list_items`, and they reconcile at the DISPLAY layer — never by writing a `list_item_contributors` row from the meal path.** Doing so would conflate "a person put this here" with "a meal put this here" and corrupt the user ledger the way the missing quantity corrupted the count. **Cross-cutting: any future work touching either ledger must treat them as a known pair.** Quantity, display and sync all respect it. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Shared-list provenance line rule:** catalog facet first (clay `#A0724A`, "Added by you & Dan"), meal facet second (teal `#0D9488`, "For Taco Night"), **teal signals meal involvement and nothing else**, each facet on its own line so no name bleeds across origins, multi-meal names the meals. Retires the "Multiple meals" count string and the ◆ diamond — color and the "Added by"/"For" wording carry the signal. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Meal-facet person name DROPPED rather than shipped wrong.** It rendered `meals.created_by` — the meal's AUTHOR, not who added it to this week's list — and that distinction isn't in the schema (`list_item_meals` has no user column). Naming the author misattributes on a shared list, so the clause is off until `list_item_meals.added_by` lands post-Vegas. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Prod promotion of meals HELD to next session, deliberately.** The demo runs on dev, so promotion is orthogonal to Vegas and the migration-sequencing risk is not worth a tired call. **Required order when it happens: `039` onto prod FIRST, then `dev→main`** — code before column 400s real users. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Recipe-card and Home-tab (activity home) design threads mocked to brand tokens — captured as DESIGN, not spec'd or scoped.** Nothing is built and no build is committed; two decisions are worth preserving regardless. **(a) meal→recipe navigation:** the fast-path meal card stays **add-capable**, and the recipe card is **read-only**, reachable from it via a "Recipe" link — the recipe card has **no add action of its own**, so there is only ever **one place that changes what's on the list**. **(b) the Home tab's "Tonight" card:** the planned meal plus its provenance chip surfaced on the front door. Token-accurate mocks: `docs/mockups/mockup_recipe_card.html`, `mockup_meal_to_recipe_flow.html`, `mockup_home_activity.html`; reference-quality renders under `docs/mockups/reference/`. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Recipe provenance surfaces as a chip ("From OurChef · [source]")** on the recipe card and on Home's Tonight card — **the visible seam** where an OurProvisions meal (a copy) traces back to an OurChef reference. **Sketched, NOT designed or scoped:** a future `recipe_cards` table (or an OurChef-owned schema once that vessel exists) plus **`meals.origin_recipe_id`** (nullable FK) to tell a traced meal from a from-scratch one. Parked here rather than in `ARCHITECTURE.md` because **nothing structural changed** — no table, no column, no migration. |
| 2026-08-20 | **"Loop of care" — recipe provenance as a RELATIONSHIP, not merely attribution — preserved as a named idea and explicitly NOT an OurChef content feature.** It needs lightweight **cross-household identity** (the giver usually isn't a member of the household doing the cooking), which places it at **Harbour scale**. Not designed further; parked until Harbour work begins. **Full text written into `docs/strategy/STRATEGY_consumption_signal.md`** (meal-intelligence section) this session: a *passive* signal (the giver can look) vs. an *active* one (the giver is notified) — the active version being the more beautiful product and the materially bigger commitment. |
| 2026-08-20 | **HOME tab v1.0 (static, no new data) → NEXT, sequenced strictly after** meal-planning v1 is demo-stable **and** the prod-promotion call is made. First cut: greeting + hardcoded shortcuts, **no live activity feed, no new table, no schema risk**. **The "Tonight" card is the one piece worth keeping even in a stripped v1.0** — the strongest story-telling surface in the app. |
| 2026-08-20 | **MEAL-PLANNING v1 SCOPE LOCKED — the elaborate version was talked back down, not abandoned.** Two PLAN sub-tabs (**Meals \| This Week**). **Add = plan = shop, one tap.** This Week is freeform draggable with a **shared** arrangement. Remove-from-This-Week **zeroes the meal's ingredients**, mirroring the existing catalog add-then-zero gesture. **The core simplification: "in This Week" is DERIVED, not stored** — a meal is in the week iff it has ≥1 unbought ingredient live on the list. **No new table in v1; This Week is a query, not a set.** |
| 2026-08-20 | **"Active" redefined as DERIVED and UNBOUGHT-ONLY — load-bearing, not cosmetic.** A meal is active iff ≥1 of its ingredients is on the list and **still pending**; bought items do not count. **That definition is what makes meal-removal safe:** removal can only ever touch unbought items, so it is structurally incapable of un-buying something another household member already purchased. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Deferred to v2+ WITH REASONS, not dropped:** days/calendar toggle, no-shop meal types (leftovers/restaurant), all-week/unassigned section, batch shop-the-week, plan/list divergence reconciliation, shared-ingredient quantity accounting. **All six were mocked before being deferred**, and each layers onto v1 without rebuilding it. v2 will likely need a real `plan_occasions`-style table; **v1 deliberately avoids one**, because the derived-active approach carries it with no new schema. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Delete-from-catalog folded into the v1 spec**, closing the P0 gap opened 2026-08-19. `deleteMeal` removes the *recipe* — a different act from removing a meal from This Week; both exist, and conflating them was the earlier confusion. ⚠️ **The phantom-badge landmine still stands:** `fetchMealProvenance` does not filter `meals.deleted_at`, so a naive soft-delete leaves the Shop badge naming a meal that no longer exists. **Fix the read path first (recommended) or choose hard-delete deliberately.** Active meals get ingredients zeroed before delete; a confirm step is required. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Create/edit meal shipped and verified on dev — DEV ONLY, not promoted to `main`.** One parameterized sheet for both modes; `updateMeal` mirrors `createMeal`'s plain-write shape. **PLAN is no longer fixture-dependent.** |
| 2026-08-20 | **`SwipeToRemove` generalized to an N-action wrapper — width counted by HANDLERS PASSED, not buttons rendered.** That distinction is the whole safety of the change: `canEdit` is false for global seed rows when prices are hidden, so counting *rendered* buttons would have shrunk those panels 240→160 and quietly altered a surface this work was never meant to touch. Every existing catalog call site stays at exactly 240. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Meals can be saved with zero ingredients.** `canSave` previously required an ingredient in create but not in edit — so an empty meal was **unreachable by creating one and reachable by emptying one**: the same end state behind two different rules. "Name it now, fill it in later" is a real workflow, so the requirement dropped rather than being added to edit. |
| 2026-08-20 | **⚠️ NEW RULE — a deploy marker is only evidence if it is ABSENT BEFORE and PRESENT AFTER.** Three false "deployed" reports this session traced to markers that already existed in the bundle (`Pet Supplies` and `Name the new category` from Browse's panel; `1.5px solid #c8973a` from `.price-input`), so they returned hits against the **old** build. **Copying an existing pattern almost guarantees the obvious markers are already present — precisely when the check is most likely to produce a false pass.** Validate both directions against the currently-served bundle before trusting a poll. |
| 2026-08-19 | **⚠️ `remove_meal_from_list` is explicitly OUT of create-meal v1 — on evidence, not on schedule pressure.** `list_item_meals` is **presence-only**: a join proving a meal touched an item, never *how much* of that item's quantity came from which meal. A concrete concurrent-edit case — two members independently decrementing one shared ingredient two meals both contributed to — **defeats both remediations `SPEC_meals_model.md`'s Open Question #1 has proposed since 2026-07-28.** Storing contributed quantity on the join row **goes stale the instant anyone touches the plain manual stepper**, which is meal-unaware by design; recomputing from `meal_ingredients` at removal time **cannot know how much of the pooled quantity still exists** by then. **Interim mechanism: Shop's existing manual per-item decrement** — honest precisely because it claims no meal-awareness it does not have. The real fix needs its own design session. |
| 2026-08-19 | **Create-meal UI conventions locked against the SHIPPED app, verified by screenshot rather than recall.** Dashed ghost-row entry terminating the meal list (matches "+ Create new place" — a header button would have created a **second competing "create new X" convention**); bottom Cancel/primary footer + small-caps eyebrow labels (matches the "Add New Item" modal); unified light-pill "Add"; search results empty until typed; inline no-results panel where **tapping a category commits create-and-add in one motion, with no separate confirm button.** |
| 2026-08-19 | **Button copy stays plain "Add" — "Add all" and "Add to Shop" both rejected.** The destination-naming problem exists **only because PLAN has no Day/Time/Occasion frame**; once that layer lands the verb resolves for free ("Add to Tuesday"). **Inventing a verb now would have solved a structural problem with copy** — the wrong instrument. |
| 2026-08-19 | **New catalog items created inside the meal-builder MUST route through the existing `insert_custom_catalog_item` path Browse uses** — never a bespoke second creation flow. One category tap both creates the catalog item and stages it into the meal draft, matching Browse's live behaviour (verified by screenshot, not assumed). Keeps *a meal ingredient is a catalog item* — the load-bearing 2026-07-29 FK decision — true at the **UI** layer, not just the schema layer. |
| 2026-08-19 | **`MEALS_ENABLED` flipped `false` → `true`.** Its original reason — `025`/`026` not live on prod, so no reachable path could touch tables that did not exist there — was **resolved 2026-08-18**. The gate's purpose is gone, so it no longer gates anything and was flipped without re-litigating it. **The constant is retained rather than deleted** so the meals surface still has a single kill switch. |
| 2026-08-19 | **The Meals lens was relocated as a TRUE relocation — a new render site under `view === "plan"` with both effects repointed — not a flag-flip in place.** This follows the standing ARCHITECTURE note that a flag-flip alone would have left the lens mounted inside Browse's render tree. **The distinction was load-bearing, not pedantic:** the two effects were keyed on `browseLens === "meals"`, so flipping the flag without repointing them would have produced a PLAN tab that rendered meal cards but never loaded meals or provenance. |
| 2026-08-19 | **`browseLens` state removed outright rather than left dormant.** Confirmed by grep it had exactly three call sites — the toggle and the two effects — all of which were being deleted or repointed, and nothing else read it. **Leaving dead state behind would also have failed the build:** `CI=true` on Vercel treats unused variables as errors, so "harmlessly dormant" is not an available option in this repo. |
| 2026-08-19 | **A deployed bundle is verified by CONTENT, never by hash.** Vercel builds with different env vars than a local build, so the served `main.*.js` hash legitimately differs from `build/` even when the source is identical — a mismatch proves nothing and a match is luck. **Evidence is marker strings:** the removed placeholder ("Plan coming soon") and the removed toggle array (`["ingredients","meals"]`) absent, the retained meals strings present. **A same-hash poll can also read as "not deployed yet" when it is actually already deployed** — that happened tonight, because the amend-and-force-push produced a tree identical to the first push, so the bundle never changed between polls. |
