| 2026-08-17 | **⚠️ CORRECTION — the "cycle violations are testing-era residue" premise from 2026-07-31 IS WRONG, and it was the premise that shrank `027`→`031` to an afternoon and got the detector dropped.** That census reported **2** stranded live `list_items`, both `Lake house`, both 2026-07-14, concluding *"every known-bad row falls inside a 48-hour window… nothing since… residue, not an active defect."* Re-measured on prod 2026-08-17: **18 stranded live rows across 4 live households** (BVI 8, Sacandaga 7, Lake house 2, Madbury 1), cycle closes spanning **2026-06-07 → 2026-08-08**. Most predate 07-31 — **the census under-counted; the data did not grow into it.** This is the same failure mode as tonight's other two corrections (the `search_path` advisor mis-attribution and the `bootstrap_new_user` overload drift): **a number measured once, quoted forward, and never re-measured.** Standing rule reinforced — **re-measure at point-of-build, never trust a census older than the decision it justifies.** |
| 2026-08-17 | **The `close_cycle` unrolled-survivor defect (Bug A) is REAL and OPEN — its 2026-07-31 deferral ("no evidence… no row anywhere shows the close-side orphaning") is reversed.** 16 of the 18 stranded rows are the close-side shape (`item.created_at < cycle.closed_at`); only the 2 Lake house rows are insert-after-close. Confirmed structurally, not inferred: the live `close_cycle(uuid, uuid[])` closes the cycle and, when `p_roll_item_ids` is empty, `return null`s immediately — unrolled live items keep pointing at the cycle just closed, **on both branches**. **Newest instance 2026-08-08 — nine days before this build, so it is actively firing, not historical.** Tracked as its own NEXT item; deliberately **not** folded into `038`, and **not** designed tonight. |
| 2026-08-17 | **`038`, not `031` — the point-of-build numbering rule caught the SAME lapse twice.** `SPEC_cycle_integrity` claimed `031` on 2026-07-31 (high-water mark then `030`), was not built that day, and `032`–`037` shipped in the interval. `031` was never created, exactly as `027` never was. At build time the mark is `037`, so this is **`038`**; filing it as `031` would sort below `032` and imply an application order that never happened. `031` joins `009`–`012`, `017`, `023`, `027` as documented drift. **Lesson recorded: a design-time migration number in a spec TITLE is a liability** — this spec's own §A predicted its renumbering and was still followed a day late. |
| 2026-08-17 | **`038` repairs 2 of the 18 stranded rows, on purpose — repair only what the same batch structurally fixes.** The 2 Lake house rows are insert-after-close, whose cause `026` closes **in this same batch**, so repair and fix land together. The other 16 come from the still-open `close_cycle` defect: repointing them would **erase the primary evidence of a live bug** and be re-dirtied on the next close with unrolled items. **New standing rule — do not clean up residue from a defect you have not yet closed; the dirty rows are the evidence.** No user impact either way: `get_list_items_for_household` never reads `cycle_id`. |
| 2026-08-17 | **The entire `SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md` series is CLOSED** — Parts 0, 1, 2, 3, 4a, 4b, 5, on dev **and** prod, every part verified from outside the SQL editor. Spec status header moved Active → closed. |
| 2026-08-17 | **`create_household(p_name, p_clerk_id)` → NEW authorization item, NEXT.** Same defect class as Part 2 (client-supplied identity on an `anon`-executable SECURITY DEFINER function, no account needed to exploit). **Deliberately NOT folded into Part 2** — it gets its own scoped migration rather than widening a spec that was closing cleanly. |
| 2026-08-17 | **21 remaining `anon`-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions on prod → NEW item, LATER — needs a dedicated audit, not reactive spot-fixes.** Includes `delete_household`, `remove_member`, `leave_household`. Fixing them one at a time as each surfaces is how the class stays open for months. |
| 2026-08-17 | **Zero-downtime pattern for RPC *signature* changes: additive migration → client deploy → removal migration.** Dropping and recreating in one step makes every in-flight client call return `PGRST202`; for `bootstrap_new_user` that is every **sign-in**, not just signups. **The intermediate state looks finished and is not** — the hardened function exists while the old vulnerable signature is still live, so never park between steps across sessions. Corollary: `drop function if exists` makes ONE file portable across drifted environments (prod dropped three real legacy overloads; dev no-opped) — prefer that over environment-specific migrations, which create exactly the asymmetry that later reads as an unexplained gap. |
| 2026-08-17 | **The trivially-true verification check is a trap — a check that passes identically before and after proves nothing.** An anon call carrying only the *safe* parameter returns `42501` either way, because the hardened function was never granted to `anon`. The evidence is the **forged** parameter shape against a throwaway identity value that matches no live row (exercises the path without mutating anyone), **paired with a control call** firing the identical forged shape at a still-vulnerable sibling — proving the resulting error is signature-specific, not a broken-RPC false negative. Same family as `033`'s "unchanged IS the pass condition" and `022`'s "must be checked from outside the database". |
| 2026-08-17 | **Dropping a policy an RPC depends on requires verifying TWO facts first, not one.** A SECURITY DEFINER function bypasses RLS only if its owner is exempt — which needs table ownership **and** `relforcerowsecurity = false`. Both were checked before `035`; had FORCE RLS been set, dropping `household_members_insert` would have locked every user out of joining a household. |
| 2026-08-17 | **`sb_publishable_` keys reject `Authorization: Bearer <key>` on direct PostgREST RPC calls**, returning a bare `Invalid API key` that reads as an authorization result but is a request-shape error. Send `apikey` alone, and control-test with an unrelated `GET` before trusting any negative. This also retires the ARCHITECTURE claim that the legacy anon key format is required. |
| 2026-08-17 | **A dormant policy is a design decision someone already made — read the catalog before authoring policy SQL.** Part 4b's spec specced nine new policies on the assumption all three tables were policy-free; `known_stores` and `shopping_sessions` each already had three, dormant only because RLS was off, authored in `archive/005` and repaired by `014`. **Permissive policies on the same command are OR'd, so the widest wins** — creating the spec'd set alongside them would have defeated both `deleted_at` filters and silently widened a deliberate per-user write rule to household-wide. Preserved the existing design instead. **Grep the live catalog before writing policy SQL, exactly as the BUILD rules say to grep before editing code** — the spec had read `pg_policy` for `household_members` and `list_items` but never for the three tables Part 4 was about. |
| 2026-08-17 | **A view does not inherit the caller's RLS — that belief was wrong in three places and shipped from the baseline.** With no `security_invoker`, a view runs with the **owner's** privileges; owner was `postgres`, which also owns `catalog_items`, and `relforcerowsecurity` is false, so `category_avg_prices` bypassed RLS entirely. **`000_canonical_baseline.sql` was the source** — its comment claimed RLS inheritance and its body named the wrong table, and ARCHITECTURE inherited both errors. Corrected `000` **directly**, a deliberate exception to "never edit fixes back into `000`", because the rule exists so the baseline mirrors reality — and here the old text was both vulnerable *and* no longer reality. |
| 2026-08-17 | **The safer definition can be the undocumented one — verify drift before "restoring canonical."** `category_avg_prices` had different bodies in dev and prod, with prod's applied out-of-band and absent from every migration on disk. The instinct to revert prod to the baseline would have been **actively harmful**: the baseline aggregates `list_items.price_per_unit`, and prod's 63 priced rows belong to **exactly one household**, so it would have served one household's real shopping prices to anon with no aggregation cover. Dev looked clean only because it holds zero priced rows. **Drift is a question, not a verdict — establish which side is safer before choosing a direction.** |
| 2026-08-17 | **`security_invoker` and an explicit predicate close different halves.** Invoker alone would make the average **household-varying** (each household's own priced custom items blending in via `catalog_items_select`), turning one consistent fallback estimate into a per-household one; the `is_global` filter alone leaves the owner-privileged bypass intact. **RLS is the backstop; the query asks for what it wants** — same principle as the `022` catalog fix. |
| 2026-08-17 | **Unchanged can be the pass condition — say so before verifying, or a null result reads as failure.** All 27 contributing prod rows were already `is_global`, so `033`'s correct outcome was an anon read returning the *identical* 5 categories and values before and after. A row-count **drop** would have meant breakage, not success. |
| 2026-08-17 | **PLAN confirmed as a standing fourth tab** (HOME/PLAN/BROWSE/SHOP) — closes the 2026-07-28 "3-vs-4 doors" open question. Driven by beta-tester feedback wanting the catalog kept pure. |
| 2026-08-17 | **Meals lens relocates from BROWSE to PLAN.** BROWSE reverts to single-lens (items + categories, no lens switching). Data model unaffected — `SPEC_meals_model.md`/`SPEC_meal_sharing.md` hold; only UI placement changes. The already-built, flagged-off lens code (`MEALS_ENABLED=false`, `browseLens` state scoped under Browse's view) needs relocating to a new PLAN view case **at build time, not just a flag flip** — caught before the flag ever went live. |
| 2026-08-17 | **MCP clients bind their credentials at session start.** An OAuth grant completed mid-session is never picked up retroactively — a full restart is required. Three sessions were lost to this. Corollary already in force: **prove reach with `system_identifier`, not a server label.** |
| 2026-08-15 | **A migration's applied-to date is a property of the database, not of the session log — read it from the database.** The 2026-07-19 entry recorded 024 as "dev-only, prod not touched"; prod `storage.objects` shows the `household-photos` bucket holding its first object at **19:58:32 UTC that same evening**. The log was wrong at the moment it was written, and **four weeks of downstream docs inherited it** — ARCHITECTURE's columns section, its migration table, its OurBanner note, and the ROADMAP row all said "prod pending" about a feature running in production. Consequence: the OurBanner storage policies sat **unverified on prod** the whole time (member-gated with no anon policy, so not an exposure — but a live surface everyone believed was dev-only). **For any migration, cite an artifact the database can produce** — an object timestamp, `pg_proc` definition, `information_schema` row — not the session that intended to apply it. Same family as "gate on a query, never on a doc." |
| 2026-08-15 | **A retire has two shapes, and the shape is decided by whose household the data lives in.** Own household → full inner-to-outer chain. Data inside someone else's household → **mangle only**. Deleting a revoked membership or an accepted invite inside another household edits *their* history to tidy up `public.users`. The retire spec describes only the first shape. |
| 2026-08-15 | **The `users` row is never deleted, only mangled — and that choice is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** 16 FK columns reference `users`, every one `NO ACTION`. Keeping the row satisfies all of them. The mangle is what *frees* the unique `email` and `clerk_id` values so the person can return; without it a soft-deleted user permanently blocks their own comeback. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Mangle by prefixing, not overwriting** — `retired-YYYY-MM-DD-<original>`. Uniqueness holds, the value is freed, and the row stays self-documenting: a year later `where email ilike '%jean%'` still finds it and explains itself. A random token leaves an unidentifiable orphan. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Clerk and Postgres are two stores, and retiring a user is two operations.** Deleting a Clerk user does not touch the database. Doing one without the other is worse than doing neither: it leaves a live prod row that hard-errors on its owner's return. Nine users were in exactly that state. |
| 2026-08-15 | **A migration roster is not a row count.** The "12 keepers" was a curated list of who to re-create in Clerk; the flip was gated on it as though it described the database. It was wrong by nine rows for three weeks. **Gate on a query, never on a doc** — the corollary to "truth is a query against the correct environment." |
| 2026-08-15 | **Add the new auth provider alongside the old one; never delete first.** Supabase Third-Party Auth accepts multiple Clerk connections simultaneously, so both issuers are honoured at once. This removes the downtime window from the cutover entirely and reduces rollback to redeploying the previous build. **Adopt as the fleet-wide pattern for any future issuer change.** |
| 2026-08-15 | **Machine-generate identifier lists; never transcribe them.** Clerk subs mix `l`/`I`/`1` and `O`/`0`. The VALUES block was built by Excel formula (`="('"&E2&"'::uuid,'"&H2&"'),"`). A transposed character produces a row that updates cleanly and then denies that user every query, silently, forever. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Verify a rowcount before committing, and make the expected number something you derived tonight.** Every destructive statement carried `and deleted_at is null` so re-running is a no-op, and each transaction had a stated expected count. The one count inherited from a doc (12) was the one that was wrong. |
| 2026-08-15 | **A user with zero live memberships is not automatically harmless.** Michael was excluded from the gate on that basis, then turned out to hold a membership row in Sacandaga — revoked, but only confirmed by querying. "Doesn't gate" and "nothing to check" are different claims. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Never enumerate a teardown surface from a cached column inventory.** `Supabase_Snippet_Public_Schema_Column_Inventory.csv` covers 11 tables and is missing at least six live ones; it produced a query against a column that does not exist. Ask `information_schema` for the FK map instead — it is authoritative and reports delete rules too. |
| 2026-08-15 | **A bundle grep for `pk_test_` is not a valid cutover check on its own.** The Clerk SDK ships both prefixes as string constants (`const rf="pk_live_",nf="pk_test_"`), so the literal appears in a correctly-cut-over prod bundle. **Match the key *with its payload* and base64-decode it** — the prod bundle's `pk_live_…` decodes to `clerk.ourprovisions.velayo.ai$`, dev's `pk_test_…` to `many-puma-34.clerk.accounts.dev$`. Verifying against a same-build control (dev preview) is what makes the result trustworthy. Correct `SPEC_prod_clerk_instance.md` step 7's checkbox accordingly. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Content Loop chartered as a reusable four-role agent pipeline (Content_Idea → Copy Drafter → Editor → Publisher)**, gated by two human checkpoints instantiating the existing Growth rule "draft, never send." Publisher is the only role that ever sends, and only immediately downstream of the final gate. |
| 2026-08-15 | **The `Ritual → Seat → Agent` ladder is REPLACED, not amended.** `docs/AGENTS.md` §1 has carried a ladder since 2026-08-05 — the design chat's premise that the vocabulary was "used but never documented" was **wrong**, which changed the work from writing a definition to superseding one. Tier now turns on **autonomous trigger + judgment + independent observation surface — all three**; the old documentation-centric definitions and the gate-vs-assembly-line promotion line are withdrawn, not held alongside. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Fan-out is part of the same Seat, and scheduling does not promote.** Tier is a property of the whole chain's root trigger, not of each link — a tool chaining through twenty sub-steps is one Seat. And **a Seat put on a schedule is still a Seat**: removing the keystroke does not add judgment. These are the two most available ways to inflate the leverage gauge, and both are now closed. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Inspector is demoted from "Agent #1 (in promotion)" to Seat.** It fails two of three conditions: deterministic pass/fail is a fixed predetermined sequence *by design* (no judgment), and it is wired into `dev→main` as a synchronous gate step (no observation surface). Its old promotion criterion would have cleared the autonomous trigger alone — and one of three is a Seat. **Net gauge effect zero** (it was in promotion, never promoted), but the Dev group now has **no Agent candidate**, which the old ladder concealed. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Analyst L1 is an Agent — bounded choice is still choice.** Selecting a restore action from a named list, on observed conditions, counts as judgment. **Classification only:** it remains Charter-tier, unbuilt, and staffed last, gated behind Watchman's signals being proven trustworthy. It is the first role to *qualify* as an Agent and will be the last one built — the distance between those two statements is deliberate. |
| 2026-08-15 | **The Agent tier lives in Ops and Growth, not Dev.** The roles that qualify or nearly qualify — Watchman, Analyst L1, Content_Idea — are all **observers** by construction. Dev's roles are **steps in a chain**, and a step in a chain fails the observation-surface condition however well it is built. A finding about the shape of the work, not the quality of the charters. |
| 2026-08-15 | **The counting rule now carries the ladder's three conditions verbatim.** `AGENTS.md` §2 previously counted any task completing "with no human between its steps" — which a **scheduled Seat** satisfies, contradicting §1 and §2's own "Seats count zero" line. The new ladder created that conflict (under the old one, running unattended *was* the promotion test). **A task counts only if the job doing it is an Agent.** |
| 2026-08-15 | **"Allowlist, not judgment" becomes "Allowlist-constrained judgment."** The old wording read as denying that bounded choice is judgment at all, which would have barred the very role (Analyst L1) it governs from a tier it qualifies for. The rule's job is to **gate what an agent may do, not to define away the choosing**. Scope narrowed, strictness unchanged: the allowlist stays closed and unrecognised signals still escalate. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Watchers are built standalone from day one, never grafted inside a Seat's script.** A grafted watcher fails the observation-surface condition **structurally** — no amount of later scheduling repairs it, only a rewrite. Built standalone, promotion to Agent is a **config change**: remove the keystroke gate, nothing else moves. Content_Idea already satisfies judgment and independent observation; the autonomous trigger is the only missing piece. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Charters are reusable; instances are parameterized per project.** The Content Loop charter is written once, generically; each project (Velayo OS, OurProvisions, OurKeep once unblocked) runs its own instance with its own Content_Idea, paired to that project's own SESSION END, since `project_knowledge_search` is project-scoped. **Role names stay fixed across instances** — no per-app renaming. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Content_Idea's default output is silence — escalation is the exception, never a signal to invent content.** Three consecutive "nothing found" passes is a correct result, not a failure. This is also what makes eventual Agent-tier promotion low-risk: an automatic watcher that stays silent most of the time isn't noise. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Content_Idea triggers only on SESSION END, never on a schedule.** The docs it reads only change at SESSION END, so a timer would re-check an unchanged door and reliably find nothing. Also avoids introducing the fleet's first timer-based compute dependency (velayo-os is static push-to-deploy today) before it is proven necessary. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Content_Idea has two distinct signal sources, not one stretched across channels.** DECISIONS LOG entries (philosophical/technical insight) feed blog and LinkedIn. ROADMAP DONE stamps + ExD-tagged entries (shipped features, craft moments) feed Instagram — a genuinely more frequent stream matching Instagram's tighter cadence. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Publisher is the cadence-keeper, not just a pusher.** Approved content drops into a channel-tagged ready backlog; Publisher drips at per-channel ceilings (blog ≤30d, LinkedIn ≤10d, Instagram ≤3d) — **maximums, never floors**. An empty backlog means Publisher skips silently rather than manufacturing a post: the same "never invent to fill a gap" discipline Editor already follows. |
| 2026-08-15 | **Instagram's visual seam resolves at the final gate, not as a separate step.** Copy Drafter and Editor stay text-only throughout. At final approval, Dan attaches/approves the screenshot alongside the copy in one motion — no new pre-selection pause, and Editor never has to judge image-vs-claim accuracy. |
| 2026-08-15 | **The gate queue is unified but tagged `[scope]` + `[owner]`, defaulting to Dan.** Mirrors The Harbour's existing lane-ownership pattern applied one level down to individual gate items. Adding a second human later is a value change, not a redesign. |
| 2026-08-10 | **The descriptor uses one verb, `Showing only`, in every phase.** `Filtering X` is backwards — X is what *survives* the filter, not what it removes. And the user's actual question at phase 2 is not "are filters on" but "where is the item I expected"; the sentence's presence already signals that filters are on, and **`only` signals withholding in one word** where a prefix like `Filter On:` would add a part to carry a signal the sentence already carries. |
| 2026-08-10 | **The Clear chip is cut.** First in the rail, the only dashed element on screen, it took visual priority over the categories while not being one, and displaced every real pill rightward. Deselect is tapping an active pill. Dissolves the phase-1 reachability question the spec had to argue away. |
| 2026-08-10 | **Staples stays in the rail and the descriptor is taught to name it.** Staples is `household_staples` row-presence (migration 016), not a `catalog_items.category` value — but Layer 1 was built predicate-aware from the start and works. Removing the pill would have discarded working logic and buried the dormant-column bug underneath it. |
| 2026-08-10 | **Desktop rail scrolling uses a styled scrollbar, not pager buttons.** The pagers existed only to compensate for hiding the scrollbar. The scrollbar shows position *and* affords dragging; the pagers did neither. **Prefer removing a part to adding one.** |
| 2026-08-10 | **Pointer media queries cannot be used to detect desktop.** Device-confirmed on a Surface with an attached mouse: `(hover: hover)`, `(pointer: fine)`, `(any-hover: hover)` and `(any-pointer: fine)` **all return false**. Any desktop-only affordance must key on layout facts (overflow, viewport width) or on inherently platform-differentiated behaviour (overlay scrollbars) — never on pointer type. |
| 2026-08-10 | **A permanent scrollbar track on a touchscreen laptop is acceptable; no viewport gate built.** iPhone shows no track. The Surface shows one, and both touch and mouse scroll via it. A thin clay hairline that shows position is not a defect on a device where a mouse may appear at any moment. |
| 2026-08-10 | **The first-run rail nudge is cut after being built.** Designed when the rail had no visual cue; the scrollbar now carries that job permanently and without motion. On device it read as a layout glitch rather than an invitation, and it fired ~400ms after the splash resolve, undercutting an entry deliberately tuned to be calm. |
| 2026-08-10 | **A sideways shift during load is the scrollbar, not the animation.** The startup shimmy was a page rendering shorter than the viewport, then growing past it as content loaded — Chrome adds the vertical scrollbar mid-load and the ~15px it occupies shifts a centered layout ~7px left. Fixed app-wide with `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on `html`. **Recorded as a diagnostic rule because the wrong thing was removed first:** the rail nudge was cut before the cause was found, and cutting it changed nothing. |
| 2026-08-10 | **SESSION END must verify in-sync-with-origin, not merely working-tree-clean.** Six consecutive docs-only sessions committed and never pushed, beginning the moment the last code deploy removed the natural forcing function. The cost was not only stranded work: the second machine was left unable to build, because the spec it needed had never left the first. **The check must be one that can fail** — a clean working tree is not evidence the work left the laptop. |
| 2026-08-10 | **A malformed commit subject is not worth a force-push.** `a6e94d9` ships to prod with a literal `@` subject. Force-pushing a shared branch for cosmetics establishes a reflex that will eventually be used when it matters. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Filter controls keep pill styling; they never adopt tab or underline styling.** Pills read as toggles, tabs read as one-of-N navigation. A multi-select control dressed as navigation misrepresents what it does. |
| 2026-08-09 | **No checkmark swap on active multi-select filters.** A row of identical ticks flattens the actives into each other; the **fill** carries state while the icon keeps each pill identifiable. Checkmark treatment remains valid for single-select. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Category icon maps must have a generic fallback (📦), not exhaustive coverage.** Category values are open — user-created categories already exist in prod. Treat the fallback as **design, not defensive padding**. |
| 2026-08-09 | **One descriptor slot per view; the more specific producer wins.** Filter text replaces declutter-cycle text when filters are active. Concatenation rejected — too long for one phone row, and the filter line already does the cycle line's job better. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Scroll where you're grazing, show everything where you're choosing.** Browse-layer controls may scroll; task-completion pickers must render every option. A hidden option at filing time produces misfiled items, and **catalog quality is the long-term asset**. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Context answers the question where it can.** Entering add-item from a section header pre-selects that category; the picker becomes a confirmation rather than a question. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Human/machine credential split: Password Manager for humans, Secrets Manager for agents.** Password Manager unlock requires the master password — handing that to an agent hands it the entire vault with no scoping and no granular revocation. Machine accounts authenticate on a scoped token alone and revoke independently. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Bitwarden free tier is sufficient through Inspector, Scribe, Tester.** 3 projects / 3 machine accounts. Watchman is the first paid seat — a known boundary in the agent roadmap rather than a surprise. |
| 2026-08-09 | **One machine account per agent seat, not per machine.** The token identifies the job. Revoking a compromised Inspector must not cost a laptop, and an agent moving between machines must not need a new grant. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Vercel Development scope: delete, not repoint.** With Bitwarden as the dev credential source, repointing creates two sources of truth that drift silently. An empty pull fails loudly; a stale-but-plausible file fails six weeks later and looks like a code bug. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Vercel retains ownership of Production and Preview build-time env.** It needs them at build. Bitwarden owns human retrieval and agent auth only — no Bitwarden→Vercel sync for a one-app fleet. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Fewer and weaker secrets beat better-stored ones.** Applies to Inspector (purpose-built read-only role over `service_role`) and to identity generally — consolidate accounts rather than multiplying per-service backups. |
| 2026-08-09 | **2FA answer is two hardware keys, not paper backups.** Paper exists only to cover a single-point-of-failure second factor. Two YubiKeys — one on the keyring, one in the lake safe — remove the SPOF and are portable by construction. Exactly one physical artifact remains: the Bitwarden master password, which cannot live inside the vault it opens. |
| 2026-08-09 | **`dan@velayo.ai` is the root of trust, not the vault.** That mailbox is the reset path for most of the stack, including Bitwarden. Its own recovery material belongs on the physical card, and its provider 2FA is the real perimeter. |
| 2026-08-09 | **Distribution mechanism is a correctness property, not only a security one.** The Preview whitespace corruption and the un-propagated Clerk key are both copy-paste failures. Values moved by `bws run` don't acquire invisible characters or miss a rollout. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Ritual → Seat → Agent ladder; only Agents count.** A job is promoted, never assumed. Promotion line: *the human may stand at the gate, but not on the assembly line* — reviewing a finished commit is a gate (still an agent); carrying a file between steps is the assembly line (still a seat). Scribe is therefore a Seat, not Velayo's first agent. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Counting rule: tasks not seats, binary, no partial credit.** A task counts when it would otherwise require a human, now completes with no human between its steps, and emits a checkable signal. Phrased as "would otherwise require a human" (not "used to cost Dan time") so it survives having colleagues. |
| 2026-08-05 | **The leverage gauge is company-wide, not per-app.** One human against all agent-tasks across the fleet. Consequence: the 1:20 target must rise as headcount grows, or hitting it will be mistaken for done. Lead externally with **profit per employee**, not the ratio alone — the ratio invites "so you have fewer people." |
| 2026-08-05 | **Instruments are equipment, not crew.** Detectors (Splunk Synthetics, RUM error detector, future probes) do NOT count toward the gauge; the agent that acts on their signal does. Rationale: Synthetics runs unattended and would qualify on a literal reading, but it automated no human toil. Precedent for every future detector. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Inspector is Agent #1, ahead of Scribe.** Part C static checks need no DB, no secrets, no Bitwarden — runnable today; Scribe's promotion is blocked behind Stage 1 transport AND Bitwarden. Deeper reason: the first hire should be the one that checks the others' work, and Stage 1 depends on Stage 0 catching a corrupted merge. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Literal crew naming, not nautical.** Inspector / Scribe / Tester / Fixer / Watchman / Analyst / Migrator. The apps carry the brand; the factory stays plain and self-documenting. Mixed naming is the worst option. |
| 2026-08-05 | **No "SRE agent" — split into Watchman (detect) and Analyst (diagnose).** SRE is a discipline, not a gradeable job; hired whole it has no failure condition. Remediation stays human. Watchman requires a **heartbeat** — a silent Ops agent is indistinguishable from a healthy system. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Restore ≠ repair — the rule that lets Analyst L1 have hands.** Restore returns the system to known-good and is reversible (restart worker, clear cache, re-run job, flip flag off). Repair is the irreversible boundary (edit config, change schema, deploy, touch data). L1 gets the first list only, from an explicit allowlist, one attempt then escalate. Unrecognised signal → escalate, never improvise. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Structure = lanes → squads → groups.** Lane is company org (Harbour); squad is per-arc and disposable, DevOps by construction; group (Dev/Ops/Growth) is a permanent taxonomy by trigger, clock, and blast radius. Grouping governs which RULES apply, not reporting lines. A squad needing a rule its group lacks means the group rules get amended deliberately in `AGENTS.md` — never a quiet squad exception. |
| 2026-08-05 | **File agents by blast radius, not by where the work starts.** Migrations are authored in Dev sessions but applied to prod → Migrator is Ops. Prevents "but I wrote it in a dev session" from ever loosening the prod boundary. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Charters are fleet-level; instances are app-level.** Scribe/Inspector/Watchman are structural and shared; Tester/Fixer/Analyst are instanced per app because "correct" is app-specific. Agents justify themselves on **platform economics**, NOT "find the current" — the two justifications stay separate. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Growth is a third group, with its own rule: no Growth agent addresses a human unsupervised — draft, never send.** A bad Dev agent costs a session; a bad Ops agent costs uptime; a bad Growth agent says something wrong to a real user in the founder's name, irreversibly. Growth spans two lanes (Marketing + Sales & Support). |
| 2026-08-05 | **Do not automate the founder conversations at beta scale.** At n=10, personally answering users is the highest-signal research available and produces the "we heard you" nurture beats. Growth agents take the deterministic observation; the human keeps the judgment and the contact. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Behaviour comes from telemetry; outcomes come from the database.** Funnel Inspector reads Splunk RUM + DEM custom telemetry for behaviour, Supabase for outcomes. The R2 invite pivot (the depth/thesis metric) is a `household_members` fact and must NEVER be computed from RUM — telemetry is sampled, best-effort, blockable. Disagreement → database wins, telemetry gap is a defect to file. |
| 2026-08-05 | **In-product surveys: triggered by the Funnel Inspector, authored by the founder, fired on an allowlist of named moments.** Never composed on the fly — it is the one Growth instrument that addresses a human. Restore-vs-repair discipline applied to attention. |
| 2026-08-05 | **Escalation is two-tier; on-call is a role, not a person.** SMS = wake me (app down/unusable, L1 couldn't or wasn't permitted to restore) — chosen because SMS rides the cell network and the boat/lake lack reliable data. Email digest = tell me (successful restores, one-off errors, self-resolved heartbeat misses). |
| 2026-08-05 | **Record WHY the rules shrink the gauge, not just the rules.** Three rules deliberately reduce the number (C-suite exclusion, instruments-not-crew, seats-count-zero), all adopted while it was internal and there was nothing to gain. Once the ratio is external positioning, the incentive to inflate arrives — the rationale is what holds. The rubric is the moat, not the ratio. |
| 2026-08-03 | **Cloud/mobile Claude Code sessions are code-only.** They produce branches for review and never write canonical docs; desk Claude Code remains the sole merge authority and owns SESSION END. Keeps a single writer for the log / roadmap / architecture. |
| 2026-08-03 | **A measurement that swings with an arbitrary threshold isn't a measurement.** The arc's width ratio held at ~0.83 across a wide luminance-threshold range — a real, derivable edge (→ `ARC_W_RATIO`=1.25). The vertical gap ratio swung ~10× over the same range — no edge, an eye call (→ `ARC_GAP`=0.41, tuned not derived). Know which you have before treating a number as evidence. |
| 2026-08-03 | **Prod DB state is established by `pg_get_functiondef` on the LIVE database, never by migration files.** OurProvisions applies migrations to prod out-of-band (no tracking table; files 009–012 aren't even in the tree), so the prod DB and the prod git branch legitimately diverge. Every "prod has/lacks X" claim is provisional until an observed `pg_proc` read confirms it. |

The mismatch became real only with `90d5b92`, which adds a live `join_household(text)` call in a bootstrap fallback. Same conclusion (apply-030-then-merge as one sequence), corrected reason. |
| 2026-08-03 | **Applying `030` to prod is window-free.** The current prod frontend never calls `join_household`; its only live `household_invites` op is `createInvite`'s INSERT (untouched by `030`). Dropping the uuid overload and tightening `invites_select` breaks nothing running on prod — no invite-flow outage — provided 030 lands before the new client does. |

| 2026-08-03 | **`search_path` audit is five functions, not six** (`archive_trip_items`, `close_cycle`, `get_active_cycle`, `get_household_user_ids`, `match_known_store`). | 2026-07-31 | **Part 1 = `join_household` + `invites_select` in ONE migration.** They cover the same exploit chain from opposite ends; either alone leaves a working attack. `join_household` alone leaves harvested UUIDs readable-but-worthless; `invites_select` alone leaves every already-harvested UUID valid forever. The spec's framing of the policy fix as follow-on hygiene was wrong. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Live-membership short-circuit returns success, not an error, and does NOT consume the invite.** A member re-tapping their own link — or an expired one — succeeds as a no-op. The RPC answers *"are you a member of this place,"* not *"did this call take a particular path."* Verified on dev: `accepted_at`/`accepted_by` still NULL after. |
| 2026-07-31 | **State-blind invite resolution ignores `deleted_at` too** — recorded in the migration body, not an accident of predicate placement. Decides one case: an existing live member tapping a soft-deleted link succeeds. Leaks nothing (they can already read the household), grants nothing (the short-circuit requires live membership). Revoking an invite governs who may **enter**; eviction is `remove_member`'s job. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Multi-use invites: Option A — `household_members.joined_via_invite_id`**, one nullable FK, over a `household_invite_uses` ledger. Grain is the membership, which is the grain actually queried. Accepted cost: last-join-wins attribution. Ledger revisited only if full join history is ever asked for. |

Test applied: *if this goes wrong, what do I actually do — and does the column change that?* `users.deleted_at` sitting for months with no writer is the cost of getting this wrong. |
| 2026-07-31 | **`invites_accept` UPDATE policy becomes dead** once the SECURITY DEFINER RPC owns redemption — drop it rather than leave a policy nobody can trigger. **Keep `accepted_by`/`accepted_at`** (74 rows of real prod history): stop writing them, comment as legacy first-use-only, retire later. |
| 2026-07-31 | **027 is retired as a number; the work becomes 031.** Claimed in prose five times, never written to disk, while 028/029/030 shipped. **Do not create a 027 stub** — correct the references and let the 026→028 gap join 009–012 and 017 as honest drift. **Never reconstruct a migration.** |
| 2026-07-31 | **The cycle-integrity evidence is testing-era residue, not an active defect.** All violations inside a 48-hour window 07-14→07-16; nine subsequent cycle-closes across three households produced zero new ones. Bounded by three caveats: thin post-07-16 activity, no structural prevention yet, and no demonstrated causal fix. |
| 2026-07-31 | **The 025/026 prod gate was inverted — dissolve it.** `026` prevents exactly the defect the gate cited, so withholding it preserved the unfixed insert path on prod. **025 + 026 + 031 ship to prod as one SQL batch.** Second-order: applying them removes the meals-client-vs-prod-schema hazard that makes the `main` gap dangerous. |
| 2026-07-31 | **031 drops the detector and defers the `close_cycle` sweep.** An alarm proving zero violations against 15-day-old residue demonstrates nothing, and the unique index makes it structurally unreachable; *never ship an alarm you haven't watched fire* argues **against** building it here. |
| 2026-07-31 | **`Our calendar` resolution APPROVED, and stranded rows are repointed, never tombstoned.** Safe without contacting the user because `get_list_items_for_household` does not filter by `cycle_id` — all 16 items are already visible, so the merge is accounting-only. Inverted, the same fact forbids deletion: they are live rows on a real list, so tombstoning would make items vanish from a user's screen. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Disclosure: NOT WARRANTED.** Closed against founder context (exposed `provision_cycles` rows largely testing-era) and the account-required nature of the invite chain on a 19-person beta of known individuals. Materially unlike 2026-07-18, where real user text was anon-readable. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Stopping rule adopted:** audit work is infinitely extensible — every look finds something and the queue grows faster than it closes. **Authorization work closes after Part 2**; new findings go to ROADMAP unspecced until meals ships. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Severity ordering is by credential requirement first, regression risk second.** The spec put Part 4 last because it touches seven call sites in the core loop. Once exposure was measured — 56 live prod rows readable and deletable with **no account** — that ordering inverted. Regression risk is a reason to be careful, not a reason to be slow. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Part 4 split into 4a and 4b.** Every function in both databases is SECURITY DEFINER and `relforcerowsecurity` is false everywhere, so the RPCs never consult the `anon`/`authenticated` table grants — those govern only direct PostgREST access. That makes revoking `anon` (4a) separable from enabling RLS and writing policies (4b): one statement, near-zero regression surface, closes the entire no-credential path. 4b remains required and now has a correct `is_member_of` to build on. |

Deciding it turns a dormant column into a contract: because every membership-scoped policy runs through `is_member_of`, setting that one timestamp revokes everything at once when account deletion is built. **Do not "clean up" the column.** |
| 2026-07-31 | **Cold-start household resolution orders by `hm.joined_at desc`** — most-recently-joined wins. Known wart: the revive branch resets `deleted_at` but not `joined_at`, so a leave-and-rejoin user sorts by original join time. Accepted; revisit if rejoin becomes common. (`household_members` has no `created_at`/`updated_at` in either environment.) |
| 2026-07-31 | **A dead-household invite falls through; it does not raise.** Drafted as a raise and reversed. The exception rolls back the whole transaction including the step-1 user upsert, so a new user on a stale link gets **no account** — and `useProvisions.js:319` throws before `:323` clears `sessionStorage`, so the code re-fires on every reload and the user cannot sign up at all until they clear browser storage. Falling through costs nothing. Marked in `029` as **DO NOT FIX BACK TO A RAISE.** |
| 2026-07-31 | **Part 1's `join_household` must carry the same `households.deleted_at` check.** Fixing bootstrap's invite branch without it leaves an unguarded second door into the same state. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Part 2's overload drops are safe but NOT inert — the earlier characterisation was wrong.** With parameter defaults visible, a 2-key call matches all four prod overloads and raises `function … is not unique` rather than silently landing on a legacy body; after the drops it resolves cleanly onto the modern function. Better outcome, but a real behavioural change. Correct the spec's reasoning when Part 2 is drafted. |
| 2026-07-31 | ~~**Migration 027 is claimed only in prose.**~~ **SUPERSEDED same day — see the "027 is retired as a number" entry above. Do NOT create the stub this entry proposes.** Original text retained for the record: No file exists; it is referenced in ROADMAP sequencing, in DECISIONS entries, and in forward-references embedded in the bodies of `025` and `026`. The gate blocking the meals prod deploy is a number, not a migration. Fix by writing a header-only stub `027_cycle_integrity.sql` so the claim is visible where numbers are actually assigned. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Data purge deferred and reframed as retention, not cleanup.** One-time deletion of test households solves the problem once; a retention window (soft-deleted rows recoverable for N days, then purged) solves it permanently and clears the backlog on first run. It is a fleet-level schema pattern, not an OurProvisions decision. Blockers today: prod has no backups, every FK is `NO ACTION` so purge order must be deliberate, and Test House 200's orphaned membership was the **only** row exercising the 029 defect. Deleting test *users* is separately hazardous — `users.email`/`clerk_id` are unique-not-null and `bootstrap_new_user` upserts on conflict. |
