That makes the file **load-bearing security config**, which is why it is committed: changes show up in a diff rather than silently on one laptop. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Write access stays with the human.** Claude Code reads truth; Dan applies migrations by hand through the SQL editor. Not training wheels — the cycle-integrity bugs of the last month were all writes landing where they shouldn't. |
| 2026-07-30 | **One MCP server registration per environment, environment named in the server name.** Never edit-and-swap a single entry's `project_ref`. The name appears in every tool call; the URL only appears if you open the file. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Never whitelist `Execute SQL` on the prod server.** Read-only bounds the damage to reads, but reads of production are exactly what should be visible. Per-call approval stays on. |
| 2026-07-30 | **`shopping_sessions` is household-scoped**, matching `list_items`. A session invisible to the household can't feed the consumption signal. Flagged for revisit when context generalizes to events: GPS plus per-session spend is right among housemates and wrong among near-strangers in a shared event. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Security work outranks the 027 prod gate.** Five live defects, two of which allow action with **no account at all**. 025+026 promotion waits. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Findings become a spec, not a same-session build.** Coordinated SQL + client changes, one scoped commit each, dev-green before prod. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Fix ordering 1→2→3→4, but NOT on the first-argued basis.** Part 4 is **not** subordinate to Part 1 — they cover **different attack populations** (Part 1 requires an authenticated account; Part 4 requires nothing but the bundled anon key), so Part 1 alone leaves the fully unauthenticated path open. Part 4 goes last because it is the only part touching the **core loop** (seven call sites — active-cycle resolution, cycle opens, shopping sessions), which makes the SQL-only change the **highest**-regression-risk of the four. "No client changes" reads as safer and here it isn't. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Prefer dropping a permissive policy over scoping it.** `household_members_insert` and the unused DELETE policies both go. A scoped-but-present policy invites widening later; absence states that the path is RPC-only. Corollary: **tightening `household_members_insert` alone would have closed nothing** — `join_household` is SECURITY DEFINER owned by `postgres` with `relforcerowsecurity = false`, so RLS never applies to it. |
| 2026-07-30 | **A SECURITY DEFINER function derives identity and scope from `auth.jwt()`, never from an argument.** `join_household` took a bare `p_household_id`; `bootstrap_new_user` takes `p_clerk_id`. Both anon-EXECUTE. Sweep the rest: `select proname, pg_get_function_identity_arguments(oid) from pg_proc where prosecdef;` |
| 2026-07-30 | **Supabase CLI / migration tracker deferred.** Numbered migrations applied by hand still work, and swapping migration machinery while 027 sits in the gate is the wrong moment. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Counts come from `count(*)`, never from a table listing.** `list_tables` reports `reltuples` planner estimates, stale until `ANALYZE`. Prod estimates were off by an order of magnitude (8 cycles reported vs 50 actual; 2 households vs 23) and a **disclosure decision was briefly made on them**. Generalizes: `information_schema.role_table_grants` returns empty for grants the querying role can't see — **empty is not absence**; read `pg_class.relacl` via `aclexplode` instead. Tool output that looks like an answer isn't necessarily one. |
| 2026-07-29 | **Meal FKs (`meal_ingredients.catalog_item_id` + both `list_item_meals` FKs) use `ON DELETE CASCADE`** — a documented carve-out from the NO ACTION convention. NO ACTION on catalog FKs is a **tripwire, not a policy**: `delete_custom_catalog_item` unwinds references explicitly, so NO ACTION exists to fail loudly when a new referencing table is added and the RPC isn't taught about it. A meal ingredient / provenance link is a *reference*, not list history (`household_staples` is the analog). Keeps 025 purely additive; avoids `CREATE OR REPLACE` on a SECURITY DEFINER fn whose live body drifted from `archive/006`. |
| 2026-07-29 | **Provenance dies with the row it describes.** Any path that resurrects a tombstoned `list_item` must clear `list_item_meals`. Resurrect = the row's history ended; nothing from before can still claim it. Table-general (any future provenance table off `list_items`), not meals-specific. |
| 2026-07-29 | **Resurrect integrity lives in a trigger, not per-RPC.** `updateQty`/`updatePrice` are client-direct writes no RPC change reaches — per-RPC fixes are structurally incomplete, which is exactly how the phantom-badge defect happened. The `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger is the only place every resurrect path passes. |
| 2026-07-29 | **The resurrect trigger always re-resolves `cycle_id`** to the household's open cycle rather than deferring to an inherited already-open value — on client-direct paths that value is just the tombstone's leftover, nobody chose it. After 027's unique index, "already open" and "the open cycle" are identical, making deference dead code. |
| 2026-07-29 | **The trigger sets `cycle_id = NULL` when no open cycle exists and never auto-opens** — a `list_items` trigger inserting into `provision_cycles` is an unpredictable side effect and tangles with the advisory lock + planned unique index. Auto-open stays a write-path job. Consequence: **null `cycle_id` is a legitimate state** — detectors alert on live-item-in-*closed*-cycle, never on null. |
| 2026-07-29 | **`add_meal_to_list` (and `insert_list_item`) resolve the open cycle server-side; `p_cycle_id` is a hint at most.** Prod evidence overruled the client-authoritative preference (two items inserted 6 min after their cycle closed). The RPC already resolves `household_id` and the user server-side — cycle is the same kind of fact, not a departure. |
| 2026-07-29 | **`PUBLIC`/`anon` EXECUTE revoked on `add_meal_to_list`.** Postgres grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC by default — wrong for any SECURITY DEFINER fn that bypasses RLS (the in-fn membership guard would be the only gate). Verified: anon call returns 401/`42501` before entering the body. |
| 2026-07-29 | **`add_meal_to_list` keeps its now-redundant provenance clear for one release.** Removing a guard in the same migration that adds its replacement means a trigger bug reopens the defect with nothing catching it. Removal → 027, after the trigger is watched working in prod. |
| 2026-07-29 | **027 promoted ahead of the 025/026 prod gate** — not on urgency (one prod household, dev clean) but on understanding: the add-path's fallback picks newest among open cycles, and we won't ship code that cements a state we can't explain. |
| 2026-07-29 | **Dev fixtures must be committed, re-runnable, and echo where they landed, and never default to a name that could match the wrong row.** A default that silently matches the wrong household is worse than one that matches nothing (`My Household` exists on dev, so a raise-on-no-match guard wouldn't fire). |
| 2026-07-29 | **Verification claims require evidence in hand.** A false green (recorded from a description, not output) is indistinguishable from a real one after the fact. Schema facts get resolved from `information_schema`/`pg_catalog` before SQL is written against them — the environment-over-doc rule applies to design-chat assertions too. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Meals ship with a scaling-ready schema but simple (flat-quantity) behavior.** `quantity_per_serving` + `base_servings` (=1) stored day one; the serving dial is deferred. The recipe-with-scaling option is additive later — no migration, no rewrite. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Meal ingredients are FKs to `catalog_items`, not free text** — that FK is the mechanism that lets a meal flow into `list_items`. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Same item from multiple meals = ONE live list row, badge "Multiple meals."** `uq_live_list_item` holds; the shopper doesn't care which meal a lemon is for. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Provenance via a `list_item_meals` JOIN table, not an array column** — referential integrity, no dangling meal refs, survives Phase 2. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Copy vs. reference is a PRODUCT split, not a toggle.** Copy (snapshot, drift-OK) = OurProvisions / households / places; reference (live-sync, evolving) = OurChef / people / authors. Reference-sharing is a natural OurChef signup driver. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Giving meals uses accept-first ("doorbell").** An offer creates a pending `meal_shares` row; the copy happens at ACCEPT, not at give. Protects the sacred shared list; scales past family. |
| 2026-07-28 | **User-facing verb is "give," not "share"** — matches copy semantics (leaves your hands, becomes theirs, no link, no take-backs); more accurate, not just warmer. |
| 2026-07-28 | **`created_by` on a copy PRESERVES the original author.** `created_by` = "who first created this"; `household_id` = "who owns this version." Feeds OurChef lineage. The thank-you moment fires at accept ("Send Dan a thank you?") — optional, once, never nags. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Nav = HOME / PLAN / BROWSE / SHOP.** Split "plan the week" (PLAN) from "build the list" (BROWSE) — different jobs, shouldn't share a surface; the old PLAN placeholder is absorbed. (Open: 3-vs-4 doors — does PLAN-the-week fold into HOME → HOME/BROWSE/SHOP?) |
| 2026-07-28 | **"Provision" is the umbrella verb** for the whole act (plan + build + shop), living in brand voice — NOT a tab label. It's the sum of the doors, not one door. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Household→Place is user-facing copy only; schema unchanged.** Rename schema once, later, deliberately — to `contexts` (place\|event) when Events lands — not to `places` now and `contexts` in a month. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Meal↔item is a bidirectional relationship on one shared list, not a one-way funnel.** Meals→list (planning) and list→meals ("what can we make") are the same link traversed two ways — this link is the OurChef seam. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Recipe vs. shopping instance:** the recipe is stable (per-serving amounts); the instance is recipe × servings − what-you-have. Editing the instance never edits the recipe (same shape as "hide is a lens, not an edit"). |
| 2026-07-28 | **Strategic thesis: OurProvisions' defensible asset is the household's live consumption signal, not the list.** Every feature either gathers the signal (list, receipts, meal reconcile) or spends it (provisions, meal-vote, demand-sensed delivery). |
| 2026-07-28 | **Receipts are the second sensor** (ground truth: what was bought) alongside the list (intention: what was wanted). The gap between them = impulse (bought-not-listed) + forgot (listed-not-bought). Validates the existing receipt specs' framing. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Sequencing = Meals → Plan → Receipts.** Meals first (richest new machinery — meal↔item link, servings model, origin tags; everything leans on it); Plan second (Plan feeds Meals, so Meals-as-objects must exist first — dependency-driven, not preference); Receipts third (independent of Meals/Plan, heavier, sharpens the signal rather than blocking it). |
| 2026-07-28 | **Provisions/inventory is opt-in on the SLOW-MOVING tail only.** Fast items churn too fast to track and don't need it; slow items (baking, household goods) are worth tracking and cheap to. Velocity is inferred from list history, not user-entered. |
| 2026-07-28 | **Demand-sensed delivery = a potential business, NOT a committed direction.** It changes what Velayo is (commerce/logistics vs. calm small apps). Decide consciously when the signal is strong enough; do not drift into it. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Prod Clerk cutover = Path A** — reconcile `users.clerk_id` by email after re-creating users in prod Clerk. Smallest change, matches the current schema (RLS keeps reading `sub`), unblocks fastest. Chosen over Path B (externalId), which would touch the JWT template + RLS predicate + `bootstrap_new_user` on live data. Clerk cannot transfer users dev→prod — it mints NEW subs, so users must be re-created and reconciled. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Reconcile-BEFORE-flip is MANDATORY** (not just preferred). `bootstrap_new_user` upserts `on conflict (clerk_id)`, so a post-flip login by an un-reconciled user hits the email-unique constraint and **throws** (hard error, not an empty app); reconciled users self-heal through the conflict branch. Run all remaining `UPDATE`s close to the flip. |
| 2026-07-27 | **`externalId = internal `users.id`` is the FLEET STANDARD** for every new vessel from provisioning day — captures the "never hostage to Clerk's ID" benefit at ~zero cost, without retrofitting OurProvisions under deadline. OurProvisions migrating onto externalId is the NEXT-tier follow-up + pilot. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Harbour Identity + fleet payments = named strategic direction.** One Velayo identity spanning vessels is the seam a fleet-level billing system attaches to (one human = one billing relationship across apps); stable identity seam first, billing policy iterates on top. Own design session (Platform/Harbour scope) → LATER. |
| 2026-07-27 | **Dan Test User (`daniel.l.holmes@gmail.com`) migrates to prod as a real keeper** — reverses last session's "delete/dev-only." Has 2 live memberships; reconcile by `id` (its email shares the `+testNN` stem, so email-join is ambiguous for this one row). Roster is now **12 keepers** (was 10). |
| 2026-07-25 | **Splash motion = one slow blur→sharp resolve in place (~2.5s), retiring v2's staged 3-act reveal.** Staged builds read as cheap/PowerPoint; one unhurried gesture reads as confident. The wordmark→header hand-off exit is preserved (it's the sequence's one earned travel). |
| 2026-07-25 | **Splash vertical position centers on the WORDMARK's optical center (~46%), not the group bounding box.** The lockup is bottom-weighted (arch is a thin crown), so box-centering always sat the eye low — the root cause of the persistent "too low" every past nudge fought. |
| 2026-07-25 | **Splash lockup spacing locked at arch↔wordmark = 2× wordmark↔tagline** (derived from one base gap, true visible edge-to-edge, in wordmark-heights) so the ratio can't drift when tuned. |
| 2026-07-25 | **Splash foot = typeset "VELAYO INC.", no glyph.** The full Velayo lockup is a hero/marketing asset — illegible at foot size, and its built-in crest arc doubles the OurProvisions crown (repeated-glyph failure). Also drops the `velayo-mark.png` dependency from the splash. |
| 2026-07-25 | **Canonical OurProvisions tagline = "Shop smarter. Shop faster."** (sentence case). Closes the four-way variant conflict. Smarter-first matches the fleet's series grammar (OurManifest "Plan smarter. Do more."); order encodes causality (smarter→faster); anaphora gives rhythm and avoids the Velayo "Live Better. Live Smarter." echo. Retired "Save time. Shop smarter." and "Eat better." Sentence case is deliberate — Playfair at splash size reads quieter than title case (which looks like a button). |
| 2026-07-25 | **Splash horizontal shake = a body-scroll toggle behind the fixed overlay, fixed by locking body scroll for the splash lifecycle (scoped), NOT a global `overflow-x` mask.** The overlay's own `overflow:hidden` already clips its children, so the scrollbar had to be the document behind it; a full-screen overlay shouldn't let the body scroll anyway. New cross-cutting pattern (see ARCHITECTURE). |
| 2026-07-25 | ~~**Production Vercel Clerk key must remain the `pk_test_` dev key until the full prod-Clerk cutover completes**~~ — **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-15: the cutover completed and Production is now `pk_live_`.** The rule was correct and load-bearing for the three weeks it governed. Its reasoning still holds for any future issuer move: the `src/index.js` guard checks non-empty, NOT validity, so a `pk_live_` key shipped ahead of the Supabase issuer would pass the guard and deny every authenticated query. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Provision a prod Clerk instance; the live app must not run on dev keys.** Dev Clerk is capped/"internal-only", so beta expansion requires prod auth. This is the gate in front of Beta 1 launch / audience expansion — it sits ahead of Trip Complete and Receipt Capture. Prod data behind dev auth is the defect; found in-browser (`performance` resource URLs), not from docs. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Prod Clerk = Secondary application** (`clerk.ourprovisions.velayo.ai`), NOT primary. Preserves `clerk.velayo.ai` (the parent-domain primary slot) for a future Velayo-wide / Harbour identity layer, so the first app doesn't mis-claim the company domain. Each future vessel takes `clerk.{app}.velayo.ai`. Also brands the verification email from `@ourprovisions.velayo.ai`. |
| 2026-07-24 | **The Clerk publishable key must be env-driven, not hardcoded.** `src/index.js` currently ships a `pk_test_` literal (dev keys baked into the bundle); move to `REACT_APP_CLERK_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` so the build env selects the key, with a missing-key guard (fail loud, not a blank white screen). |
| 2026-07-24 | **Migrate 10 real users, drop 11 test accounts.** Prod starts empty; migration is opt-in. Keepers are the beta cohort + family + 2 dormant-but-real (real prod data); drops are the `daniel.l.holmes+testNN@` alias set + empty/never-bootstrapped accounts. **`clerk_id`/`sub` continuity is load-bearing** — migration must preserve or reconcile it per user or prod `household_members` orphan. |
| 2026-07-24 | **Google OAuth: External + published, basic scopes only.** External (not Internal — testers are Gmail, not `@velayo.ai`); basic scopes (openid/email/profile) mean no verification review, no 100-user cap, no unverified-app warning screen. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Wave audio and the BVI water wash move from the splash to trip completion.** At launch the wash washes away an *introduction*, which is incoherent; at completion it washes away the actual finished list, which is literal and earned. The reward lands on effort, not on opening an app. Supersedes the 2026-07-22 "dissolve = BVI wash" and "single wave on the dissolve" splash decisions — both are relocated, not deleted, and need a Trip Complete spec to claim them. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Tap gate removed from the entry; the splash auto-plays on cold start.** The "Tap to enter" gesture existed solely to satisfy browser autoplay policy for the wave. With audio gone it is pure friction. The gesture audio needs is supplied later by the existing "Close & clear" confirmation at trip completion. Supersedes §5's entry-tap. |
| 2026-07-23 | **Entry sequence is grouped by meaning, not visual staging: horizon (the world) → vessel (*Our*Provisions + tagline) → house (arch + Velayo colophon).** The arch belongs with the house — it is part of the Velayo logo — despite sitting spatially *over* the wordmark ("the house setting its mark above the vessel"). |
| 2026-07-23 | **Wordmark enters in place (opacity + blur resolve), not by sliding up.** The slide worked when audio carried it and it rhymed with the hand-off (which also rises); emerging in place makes the **hand-off the only travel in the sequence**, so the movement means something when it finally happens. |
| 2026-07-23 | **The starfish parable does not belong on the splash** — it needs reading time the threshold cannot give. Candidate home: the **empty-list state**, which currently has room and nothing in it (→ LATER). |
| 2026-07-22 | **Splash is a vessel-specific launch *experience*, not a generic screen — and it sets the fleet-wide pattern.** Warm app palette + product wordmark lead + a quiet Velayo footer + a dissolve into the app. Every future vessel (OurKeep, OurManifest, OurDiscovery…) inherits it. The parent brand signs the footer only. |
| 2026-07-22 | **Splash palette = OurProvisions espresso `#2C1A0E`, NOT Velayo navy.** The old splash showed the Velayo brand even though the user opened OurProvisions — a front-door mismatch. The vessel owns its own splash; Velayo is a quiet `VELAYO INC.` colophon at the bottom. |
| 2026-07-22 | **Dissolve = a BVI turquoise water wash** (the actual place — "ocean in your hand"), rejecting sunset-gold. A cool palette works *because* it's a tight, harmonious color band (the same cohesion lesson as the warm band; band locked in `bvi_palette.png`). The ocean literally washes the view away to reveal the app. |
| 2026-07-22 | **Splash sound = a single wave breaking on the dissolve**, triggered by the entry tap (satisfies browser autoplay), cold-start only, respects the mute switch. Rejected a chime (too "notification") and an anchor-clank (synthesis can't render heavy chain convincingly — use a real recording if ever revived). Minimal, and tied to the only moment you see water. |
| 2026-07-22 | **Arch geometry locked by Dan's arch-matched logo-overlay test: arch width = 0.52 × wordmark width, "higher" placement.** A proportional rule so it holds at any size (~124px at the 238px wordmark). Earlier 94/156px + tucked/stranded placements were rejected. Position it absolutely (not in the wordmark's flex column, or the gap collapses). Ref `h_high.png`. |
| 2026-07-22 | **The experience must carry into the app — a threshold that lies is worse than no threshold.** The espresso header retains a faint permanent BVI depth-glow; app interactions share the scene's easing `cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)`. This is a real scope expansion — likely its own fast-follow build phase, not shipped with the first splash. |
| 2026-07-22 | **Build the splash experience Phase 1 in React/PWA → NEXT.** Design is fully specced + proven in the `SPLASH_final.html` mockup; the build (splash scene + BVI wash + wave sound + hand-off to the header) is the next engineering step. |
| 2026-07-21 | **Feedback channel = Google Group (thoughts@velayo.ai), not a personal alias.** Shared, threaded, crew-addable, reusable across the Harbour fleet, outlives the beta. **External posting is the load-bearing setting** — verified by an arriving email, not the settings screen. Distinct from the in-app "Message the bridge" feedback table planned in Beat 0. |
| 2026-07-21 | **The Velayo welcome email is a COMPANY welcome, not a product welcome.** No OurProvisions-specific caveats (e.g. "rough edges") in it — product-level honesty lives in the OurProvisions welcome / weekly email. |
| 2026-07-21 | **Brand thesis line locked:** *"Velayo builds small, beautiful apps that remove the little daily frictions that quietly steal your time, energy, and peace."* Two independent reviewers crowned it. Propagate to homepage, deck, email signature. |
| 2026-07-21 | **AI is a method, never the lead** — *"they use AI, but the technology is never the point."* Reinforces the "craft company that happens to use AI" identity. |
| 2026-07-21 | **Harbour metaphor = refuge, not confinement: *"safe but never stuck."*** The harbour is the sanctuary the fleet serves; the reader is protected but always free to sail. Founding-principle-level idea for the About page. |
| 2026-07-21 | **Add an "About page / founder's letter" to the backlog.** This session generated the full starfish parable, the long-form two-part philosophy, and the harbour-refuge principle — good writing without a current home; pair with the `ourprovisions.app` story surface. |
| 2026-07-19 | **Nav seam = gradient dissolve (Option 1), and the header + nav share ONE continuous photo+gradient.** The espresso nav was a solid block butted against the photo — a hard seam. Chosen over Option 3 (translucent floating nav with blur). Implementation fact worth keeping: **you cannot align a photo across two separate elements** with percentage `background-position` without the image's aspect ratio (vertical % resolves against each element's own height), so the only clean continuity is a single background spanning both — a relative wrapper around header + nav, both transparent over it, gradient ending at solid `#2C1A0E` so the tabs keep full contrast where it's opaque. |
| 2026-07-19 | **A programmatically-clicked hidden `<input>` bubbles its synthetic click to ancestor `onClick` handlers.** The photo-upload "silent no-op" was `fileInput.click()` dispatching a click that bubbled from the input (a child of the sheet backdrop) up to the backdrop's `onClick={close}`, unmounting the sheet mid-pick — no upload, no throw, no log. Rule: nest a hidden file input **inside** the `stopPropagation` container (or stop propagation on it), never directly under a dismiss-on-click backdrop. General lesson: a picker-opens-but-handler-silent failure is often a detached/unmounted handler, not a swallowed exception. |
| 2026-07-19 | **Wordmark default is Large, and the wordmark control is photo-gated (confirmed at build).** Small and Hidden are available; the segment renders only when a photo exists. Keeps the empty-state header honest and makes the empty-band case unreachable (reaffirms the 2026-07-18 "banner control only exists when a photo exists"). |
| 2026-07-19 | **Leave household stays in the membership zone for non-creators; Delete stays creator-only inside the Edit sheet.** The spec was silent on Leave. The entity/membership cut resolves it: leaving is *you acting on your own membership* — a people action, so it belongs below with the roster/Invite, not with the entity's Edit/Delete. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Household management adopts the entity/membership model (collection → selected → scoped detail).** Household CRUD above the line, this-household membership below, one selection driving both zones (membership recomputes on switch). Rationale: separates the household-as-entity from the people in it, and deliberately builds the structure Phase II Events reuses — a "Your Events" sibling section drops in without re-architecting. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Active-row affordance is a bare pencil (FINAL3), not a pill; the "ACTIVE" word is removed.** Selection already reads from espresso fill + clay ring + header echo; the "Edit" label carries the action without chrome, keeping the row-action pattern uniform (trash + Edit both bare, right edge). A simpler rule than "icon-only bare, labeled gets a pill." Pill variant (FINAL2) was built and rejected. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Delete household moves into the Edit sheet (danger zone, creator-only), refining `SPEC_household_photo_header.md` D2.** Under the entity model, Delete is an operation on the household and belongs with its other edit-actions — you go *into* the thing to destroy it. "Created by you" resolves to the creator-only *visibility* of Delete (D4), not a header label. Permission unchanged: creator-only. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Invite transport = the OS share sheet (`navigator.share()`); the in-app share UI and "Copy link" are removed.** The persistent banner *was* the bug; removing the in-app surface removes the defect class by construction, and simplifies the code (verify the app calls `navigator.share()`, not a self-rendered sheet). The share sheet already offers Copy. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Brand principle: maritime is expressed through language and imagery (place names, "aboard"), not repeated decorative glyphs.** Anchor/monogram placeholders dropped; household rows are name-only — the name is the identity, the photo lives in the header and Edit sheet, not repeated in the switcher. |
| 2026-07-18 | **A policy's name is not its predicate.** `"Anyone can read global catalog items"` had no `is_global` check and leaked every custom row to anon. The name documented an intent the SQL never implemented, and it survived because RLS was correct everywhere else. **Sweep `pg_policy` across all tables for names that assert filters their predicates don't contain.** |
| 2026-07-18 | **A query asks for what it wants; RLS is the backstop, not the filter.** The anon fetch requested every row and relied on RLS to decide what came back. Relying on RLS as the *primary* filter makes every policy load-bearing for correctness, not just security — and when both layers fail, the failure is silent and the blast radius is the whole table. |
| 2026-07-18 | **`catalog_items` is PII-bearing, not reference data.** Users treat free-text item names as a scratchpad — the leak exposed a parent's medication schedule and dated caregiving notes, not groceries. Any future anon or cross-household read of this table needs `list_items`-grade scrutiny. |
| 2026-07-18 | **The SQL editor cannot verify RLS.** It runs as `postgres` and bypasses policies — it returned all 183 rows before *and* would after a correct fix. Verify via `set local role anon` in-transaction, or an anon-key request through PostgREST. The editor will lie about both the policy and the data. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Keep anon browse, scoped to `is_global = true`.** Considered dropping the anon policy entirely. The signed-out browse of the 50 seed rows is a real part of the landing experience and the seed rows are Velayo's own content with no household attached. The risk was never anon browse — it was anon browse of a table that *also* holds user text. If the landing page ever needs richer data, build a purpose-made view; don't widen this policy again. |
| 2026-07-18 | **OurBanner: the group sets the look.** Photo, framing, banner state, and name are household state; any member changes any of them. Reversed an in-session lean toward a per-user preference — a private reskin of shared chrome is the one place the app would quietly stop being *ours*. Accepted cost: Helen changes it, Dan's header changes with no attribution — exactly how the household name already behaves. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Delete household stays creator-only and stays in the switcher modal.** Held deliberately against a broader "anyone can do anything" rule. Deleting isn't editing — it ends a thing three people use. Keeps `"Created by you"` meaningful, and keeps the existing `window.confirm()` at its current risk level rather than making it more dangerous. |
| 2026-07-18 | **The banner control only exists when a photo exists.** No photo → no control, wordmark renders large, empty-band case unreachable by construction. The setting appears at the moment it becomes meaningful. This rule is what let "hidden" mean hidden with no fallback lockup. |
| 2026-07-18 | **When three consecutive fixes to one idea each introduce a new defect, the idea is wrong, not the execution.** "Hidden" was designed four times: name-promotes-to-hero → long-name overflow; corner mark → legibility depended on the user's photo; relocated corner mark → displaced the tab bar. All three were downstream of the hero promotion. Removing the first domino removed the chain. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Zoom is mandatory, not polish.** A 2.6:1 header band cannot be filled by an arbitrary photo with `background-position` alone — `IMG_7897` frames correctly only around 165%. Beat 1's three fixed slices could not express this. Framing = position **and** scale, both persisted. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Assign migration numbers at point-of-build, not design time.** Third double-claim in four migrations (021 `beta_signups`/referral; 022 referral/anon-catalog; 023 referral/photo-header). Specs claim a number at design time and it moves before build. Check `migrations/` for the true high-water mark before writing any number into a file. |
| 2026-07-18 | **Referral primitive renumbers 022 → 023.** The anon catalog fix took 022 and is applied; the photo header took 024. ROADMAP prose saying "migration 022" for referral is now stale for the second time — use 023 (or the true next-free at build). |
| 2026-07-17 | **Heading and body must do different jobs.** Every awkward caption was two sales pitches stacked — heading made a claim, body made the same claim longer. The invite caption always worked because its heading is an *instruction* and its body explains. Rule: heading states, body explains, each caption owns exactly one idea. A caption carrying two stories is what reads as awkward. |
| 2026-07-17 | **Don't use a product term as a plain-English word.** "Staples" is a filter chip, a table (`household_staples`), and a user action. In a heading it reads as the button; in the body, after the plain idea lands, it reads as the name for the thing. Heading gets the person's words ("the stuff you buy every week"), body gets the feature word — teaching without a lesson. |
| 2026-07-17 | **Speed is a claim the page had never made.** The thesis frames the shop as the pain, but no caption said the app makes the trip *faster* — the most obvious thing a shopper wants. Guardrail: the app doesn't make you faster, a *list* does — so the body says "as you move" rather than boasting "record time." |
| 2026-07-17 | **Legal pages are Velayo's — one canonical copy for the fleet.** The landing footer links out to `velayo.ai/privacy` and `velayo.ai/terms` with absolute URLs rather than copying them per app. Copying legal text onto each front door is the divergence problem at 100 apps, and it starts at app one. |
| 2026-07-17 | **Footer uses `grid-template-columns:1fr auto 1fr`, not flex `space-between`.** Equal outer columns hold the middle at the page's true centre regardless of side widths; flex drifts toward whichever side is shorter. |
| 2026-07-17 | **★ Judge the live page in the real browser, not a mockup in an in-app viewer.** Four rounds were spent "fixing" a dark-mode bug that didn't exist — Claude's in-app browser was theming a downloaded HTML file. `ourprovisions.app` in Safari was correct the whole time. **Check the surface before debugging the render.** Same family as "truth is a query against the correct environment," extended to what you're *looking through*, not just what you're querying. |
| 2026-07-17 | **The privacy policy contradicts a live public promise.** The landing page says *"we read every answer"* and *"every one of them decides something real about what we make next."* The policy (effective 2026-03-23) says we collect an email address for a waitlist. Two of our own live surfaces disagreeing — not a compliance nit, and only visible to someone who reads both. |
| 2026-07-17 | **Claude drafts the schema-accurate sections; a lawyer owns the document.** The hard part of a privacy revision is knowing exactly what's collected — a good use of an assistant that knows the schema down to the column. Representations about how a Delaware corporation handles personal data are not. §07 (`fit_note` + right of access) left blank deliberately. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Do not upgrade Supabase compute.** Reverses an earlier recommendation made before seeing real IOPS. Prod runs at ~0.1% of disk IO capacity (3 of 3,000 IOPS); upgrading would have paid to fix a load problem that does not exist. Free tier / nano is adequate at current scale. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Alert on user-visible failure, not infrastructure metrics.** The outage proved it: infra metrics (Disk IO) screamed and were wrong; the user-visible signals (RUM JS errors, 522s) were quiet in an unwatched dashboard and were exactly right. Detectors go where the user lives. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Synthetics must exercise the Data API, not the homepage.** Vercel served the HTML shell happily throughout the outage — a homepage browser check would have stayed green the entire incident. The failure lived in PostgREST; the check must reach PostgREST (`GET /rest/v1/catalog_items?…` with the anon key). |
| 2026-07-15 | **Defer the Splunk OTel Collector (AWS Lightsail) to Phase 3–4.** The free 15-host tier removes the cost objection but not the attention cost (a VM to patch, a Compose file, a scrape config that fails silently). The Collector would have shown a healthy DB during the outage — real value, but not the bottleneck. RUM + Synthetics delivered ~80% of the benefit with zero operational surface. Keep the design for when it's *wanted*, not *needed*. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Single Synthetics location (AWS N. Virginia).** Matches Supabase US East. Four locations = 4× requests and 4× false-positive surface for no added signal at this scale. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Watch Supabase legacy anon/service_role key deprecation.** Legacy keys are on a path toward Publishable/Secret keys. Not urgent — the app uses the legacy anon key and the Synthetics check matches it. Revisit when Supabase forces the migration. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Founder dashboard, not intranet.** Solo founder needs a launchpad/balance instrument; Drive stays source of truth for documents. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Four-lane charter structure.** Dashboard organized around standing functional lanes (charters), not links — so agents/hires onboard into a charter that exists before headcount. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Leverage ratio definition.** 1 human : tasks run by agents, summed from per-lane agent counts. Excludes C-suite "advice" Claude projects — keeps the number honest and un-inflatable. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Three-repo separation: `velayo-os` / `velayo-platform` / app repos.** Cockpit ≠ engine. Different audience, lifecycle, blast radius. OS never ships to a customer. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Automation-first hosting.** Cloudflare + Git push-to-deploy, not drag-and-drop. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Private = secure auth, not obscure URL.** Cloudflare Access OTP over in-page PIN (PIN is client-side theater). |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Access policy: named emails + `@velayo.ai` domain rule.** Scales without per-hire edits. Coupling noted: issuing a velayo.ai email = granting Harbour access. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **SESSION END pipeline is v2.** Chat emits `design_handoff.md`; Claude Code owns the canonical write. v1 Drive-writing Scribe retired. One log, repo is source of truth. |
| Jun 11, 2026 | **Single company log for now, scope-tagged.** Stays in `ourprovisions/docs` until app #2's first session — at that point split into `velayo-os/docs` as a filter, not a migration. |
| Jul 11, 2026 | **Product specs sort into `ourprovisions/docs/specs/{active,built,retired}/`; Velayo OS process docs route to `velayo-os/docs/`.** Two-repo split keeps the product decision-trail separate from OS/workflow machinery. Lifecycle folders created this pass (were design-intent only until now) — executed in `0303397`. |
| Jul 11, 2026 | **Diagnosis→fix spec pairs both go to `built/`, not retired.** Retired means design *changed*; a two-document trail of one shipped fix is forensic record, not supersession. |
| Jul 11, 2026 | **`CLAUDE.md` stays at ourprovisions repo root** (not `docs/`) — corrects a memory error; it is already correctly placed. |
| 2026-07-12 | **Catalog naming rule:** a household custom item may NOT duplicate a global item's normalized name (reuse the global); slightly-different names ("Wheat Bread" vs "Bread") are legitimate distinct customs. Matching is exact-on-**normalized** (lower + collapse-whitespace + trim), **NEVER fuzzy** — fuzzy would destructively merge distinct names. Shapes migration 018's reuse logic + 019's index predicates. |
| 2026-07-13 | **Bug 1 is a catalog-layer fork, not list-layer** — corrected by a prod query (3 list rows → 3 distinct `catalog_item_id`s). Fix at write-time (018 reuse) + DB backstop (019 indexes), NOT by scoping the list re-add. Reaffirms "diagnose against a live prod query, never against code comments or the initial hypothesis." |
| 2026-07-13 | **Global-vs-household collision deferral (from spec) resolved: F1c built.** Cross-scope shadowing (custom shadowing a global name) is enforced by 018's write-time reuse; same-scope self-forks are enforced by 019's two partial unique indexes. The two mechanisms cover the whole naming rule together — neither alone is sufficient. |
| 2026-07-13 | **F2 keying: kept check STATE name-keyed, made the WRITE row-specific (`.eq("id", listItemId)`).** Did NOT rekey state to `list_item.id` (Option B) — not justified by KISS now that 018/019 + cleanup make "two live rows, same name" structurally impossible. Revisit id-keying only if the F0 constraint ever fails to hold. |
| 2026-07-13 | **F3 Slow-3G display flicker (brief uncheck→recheck under throttling, self-corrects) logged as a known cosmetic edge — do NOT fix.** KISS: imperceptible in normal use; fixing risks destabilizing a proven optimistic guard (`pendingCheckRef`, mirror of `pendingQtyRef`). |
| 2026-07-14 | **Referral is a distinct primitive from household-invite.** Invite grants authorization (token → `household_members` row → write access); referral grants nothing (no token, no membership — points at the front door). Conflating them forces advocates to over-share or hand-explain a URL. Capture attribution at the link layer NOW (`?ref=<code>`) — referral chains can't be reconstructed retroactively; rewards logic deferred to Phase 4. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Chris's "own list" = a lens, not a fork.** A separate list forks the household and kills CI. A personal *view* of the shared list IS CI — shared truth through one person's lens at the moment of action. Consistent with the `activeHouseholdId` pattern (client lens; server checks membership). No per-user list. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Priority/intent signaling belongs on `list_items`, not the catalog and not per-user.** The point is Heddi's "skippable" is visible to *Chris* — it crosses the person boundary intentionally, like check and remove. Do NOT model it as a per-user catalog attribute like hide. Three states max; observed poles = "must-have" (shopper owns the miss) and "skippable" (requester owns the miss). Mechanism deferred to affordances/mockup work. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Multi-household member-adding: Option B (people-picker) approved; Option A (clone household) deferred.** Once two users share any household, adding one to another is *selection, not invitation* — the invite handshake exists to establish trust between strangers; re-running it between connected users asks them to re-prove what's proven. B touches membership only, degrades to invite-link for true strangers. A (clone carrying items/staples) is unvalidated — powerful for "same life, different location," harmful for a different context. If A is built, keep it composable with B, not monolithic. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Sub-decision on Option B — instant-add, but not silent.** Instant-add is fine between already-connected users, but the added user must *see* they were added, not be silently enrolled. Adding grants write access and exposes a household's existence + contents — "any user I share one household with can silently drop me into new households" has a consent edge. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Hidden-items findability is a correctness defect, not a design preference.** An unfindable hidden item is indistinguishable from a deleted one — collapsing the two-layer hide/delete distinction the model depends on. Hide must stay findable by the person who hid it. *(Delivered by F1b 2026-07-15 for the exact-match case; grouped-hidden-in-search is the residual.)* |
| 2026-07-14 | **Granular unhide is a byproduct of the search fix, not a separate feature.** Surfacing hidden matches in search with inline per-item unhide *is* the granular-unhide path; "unhide all" demotes to a wholesale convenience. No separate unhide-manager UI needed first. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Search/filter label: do not relabel standalone.** The "Search" bar behaves as a live filter (mis-priming the hidden-banana surprise), but once search surfaces hidden items it moves back toward genuine search. Decide label + behavior together in the hidden-items work, or risk relabeling twice. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Android keyboard real-estate: watch-item, not a fix.** Soft keyboard sandwiches the filtered list. Evidence thin (one platform, two users, no blocked task); moving the primary-screen search bar for a not-yet-painful problem — and bottom-anchoring collides with the top-search convention. Gather more data first. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Event as a second provisioning context — named strategic concept.** Household = standing/permanent; event = bounded/ad-hoc/deadline-driven (golf trip, bachelorette). The event may express the CI thesis *more* vividly (acute pain, unavoidable collaboration, natural referral surface, easier on-ramp). No change to Phases 1–3. |
| 2026-07-15 | **Hide is a lens, not an edit.** Re-adding an item that is hidden-for-me but live on the household list is un-hide only — reveal the shared row at whatever quantity the household set, write nothing. Rejected un-hide+increment and un-hide+set: no one changed the quantity, so the quantity must not change. A per-user view action cannot have a household-wide side effect. |
| 2026-07-15 | **A contributor badge is the last thing you said.** Attribution comes from utterance, not arithmetic. Your badge clears only when *you* (a) reduce by the full amount you asked for, or (b) reduce to zero. Everyone else's actions change the number, never your statement. Silence is not withdrawal. |
| 2026-07-15 | **No permission walls between household members.** Anyone may set the shared quantity to anything, including past their own contribution — no blocking, no warning. "We should not need software to protect people from other people in the house." Distinct from "shared list is sacred," which guards against *category* errors (a per-user lens masquerading as a household edit), not deliberate household edits. This is CI. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Referral = advocacy, and the real reason to build it is that hand-composed links are a defect surface.** Not "spread the word." Dan texted Heddi a schemeless `ourprovisions.velayo.ai`; her Android threw "Could not find an appropriate application." A link composed by the app (`https://` + prose, via Web Share) is well-formed **by construction**; a human at midnight is not. Owning the artifact is the fix — the same reason the household invite already carries a pre-written message. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Referral lives in Preferences, NOT in the household share sheet.** Different layers: invite is household-scoped, referral is app-scoped. Co-locating forces users to notice a distinction they don't care about at the exact moment we want the harder action — "just tell them about the app" is easier than "share my list," so it cannibalizes the depth metric (≥50% invite a second person; the invite is the ONLY path to the aha). |
| 2026-07-16 | **No referral gamification. Ever-or-later, not now.** Referral volume rewards blasting the link at everyone; one referred spouse produces the entire CI thesis, twenty referred acquaintances produce nothing. A leaderboard would rank the second above the first — contradicting the locked metric's warning that *breadth without depth is a false pass*. Confirms rewards→Phase 4 (2026-07-14). |
| 2026-07-16 | **Do not track referral sends — only successful signups.** `navigator.share` hands off to the OS and reports nothing: a send is **unobservable**; only *intent* (the tap) is knowable, and it overcounts every cancel. Conversions ÷ intents yields a rate that looks terrible and means nothing. Removes a table, a counter, and a misleading ratio. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Success = a `users` row exists with `referred_by` set; `referred_by` IS the metric.** The schema picks the honest definition — a `users` row only exists if they cleared Clerk **and** bootstrapped in. Counting Clerk accounts counts ghosts (Aidan made an account and never arrived). `count(*) where referred_by = <id>` — no counter to drift, no table to maintain; attribution and measurement are the same row. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Activation tracking is NOT a referral sub-feature.** "Did they log in / wrap a trip / share a household" is the **existing activation ladder** (R1–R5, 2026-07-09) — R2 *is* "invite a second person." Build activation instrumentation once for **all** users and slice it by `referred_by`; building it inside referral means building it again for organic users next month. (Caught live: scope grew from a share button to an analytics platform in six messages — what KISS exists to catch.) |
| 2026-07-16 | **The mission belongs on the landing page, not on a button.** "Help your friends live healthier, happier, earth-kind lives" is the reason **Dan builds**, not the reason **Glen shares** — delivered from a Preferences drawer for a grocery list, the gap between the ask (text a link) and the claim (improve a life) reads as marketing. The sine-wave page (Boardroom → Trough → Ah-ha → Vision → Front Door) carries the mission; the button only has to get them there. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Referral copy is the frame around the sharer's endorsement, not a pitch they forward.** Glen isn't relaying Velayo's words — the endorsement IS Glen; the link is the artifact. Superlatives ("best list app ever built") fight the positioning and invite argument; nobody forwards a boast. |
| 2026-07-16 | **Vocabulary is load-bearing: Harbour = the shared identity layer, Fleet = the apps, Crew = people on a trip. Launch assets have been DESIGNED but UNWRITTEN since 2026-07-05/09, skipped across five build sessions. **Promote the sign-up page + questionnaire copy to NOW.** |
| 2026-07-16 | **Never reconstruct a missing migration file.** 009–012 and 017 aren't on disk; they ran and were never filed. A reconstructed migration is a guess wearing the costume of a record — worse than an honest gap, because the next session trusts it. If ever repaired, read `pg_proc.prosrc` from prod and stamp the header **"reconstructed from prod on <date>"**. Sequence gaps can't be reconstructed at all — a DB shows end state, not history. |
| 2026-07-16 | **A backup is not a backup until its TOC is verified and it is off-instance.** `pg_restore --list` must show TABLE DATA (not just schema); a dump on the same laptop is a copy, not a backup. `--no-owner --no-privileges` is correct for Supabase: platform role grants don't transplant, but RLS policies, constraints, RPCs, and realtime publications all survive. | 2026-07-16 | **CTA prominence tracks whether another CTA is visible.** The nav "Come aboard" stays a ghost while the hero's own button is on screen (two loud buttons thirty pixels apart is one too many) and solidifies once it scrolls away. | 2026-07-15 | **Don't buy infrastructure during an outage.** Backups are a real gap (Free tier, no recovery path, real beta users' data) but the Pro decision was deferred to a green dashboard — you can't verify an upgrade took while the platform is down, and reacting to an incident is how you over-provision. | June 8, 2026 | **Catalog visibility model.** Global seed list = permanent, undeletable household-wide, exists to get households started. Custom items = any member can add/delete, household-wide. **Hide** = per-user, browse-only, reversible, never touches the shared list. **Delete** = custom items only, household-wide, cascades. Global reset = separate, gated, re-seeds. Multi-item hide deferred until demand. Two principles: (1) the shared list is sacred — no per-user view preference suppresses it; (2) every removal has a proportionate undo (hide → unhide one-tap personal; custom delete → soft-delete recovery window; reset → deliberate and total). |

| Jun 12, 2026 | **`bootstrap_new_user` canonical = 4-arg form** `(p_clerk_id, p_email, p_invite_code, p_full_name)`. Prod has four overloads (ambiguity risk); dev installs only this one. |
| Jun 12, 2026 | **Category persistence works as designed — revisit only on user friction.** Categories are item-derived; an empty category disappears when its last item is deleted. Considered promoting to first-class persisted entities; deferred. Edge case affects only the creator of an empty category, not the common case. |

| Jun 12, 2026 | **Reproduce known debt AS-IS; never fold fixes into the baseline.** The baseline must match what's actually running, bugs included. Fixes go in separate, named, tested migrations so validation isn't muddied. Debt flagged inline with `-- KNOWN DEBT`. |

Migrations must be idempotent and verified against the live catalog. |

| Jul 1, 2026 | **Durable intent must also be RETRIABLE against a late-arriving source.** The `pendingJoinId` fix made intent survive across renders but still reconciled single-fire against `myHouseholds`. When the authoritative source (membership list) populates late — as it does on prod — a one-time reconcile misses and the intent strands. Pattern refinement: reconcile on *every* change of the source while the intent is unresolved, and clear the durable marker only after the target state is **confirmed**, not on the attempt. (Corrects the durable-intent decision above.) |

| Jun 17, 2026 | **Active context is client-authoritative — the Harbour standard.** Each app instance holds its own active household (context + localStorage); the server authorizes the claimed household, never picks one. | Jun 17, 2026 | **One unified "manage household" sheet.** Not separate switcher + members sheets: switch households at top, manage members/rename/invite of the active household below. | 2026-06-18 | **Use SECURITY DEFINER `is_member_of` (not inline subquery) on `household_members_select`.** Inline subquery against the same table re-triggers the policy (RLS recursion). SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS inside the function body. Recursion-safety rule: any policy that must read the table it guards must route through a SECURITY DEFINER helper. |

| 2026-06-18 | **Ship DB ahead of code, deliberately.** New policies are strictly more permissive in the correct direction; old single-household frontend operates safely over them. De-risks a large feature: migrate + prove-harmless first, then merge the frontend. |

| 2026-06-19 | **Invite-join switching is intent-gated on user state.** First household ever → auto-switch (nothing to interrupt; invite is the front door). Extends "user holds the lens / confirmation not silent switch" from location to membership. Rationale: silent join fails safe (one tap) vs. mid-work context loss; the edge case is rare and unmeasured, so deferred pending usage evidence. |

| 2026-06-20 | **insert_list_item upsert uses last-write-wins on quantity, not additive.** Additive would fight the `updateQty` UPDATE path (absolute set-value semantics); two competing semantics would cause drift. Concurrent-add race is rare enough that LWW is the safe, consistent choice. |
| 2026-06-20 | **On conflict, clear deleted_at and force status='pending'.** Concurrent add against a soft-deleted slot resurrects cleanly instead of erroring. Eliminates the Lemons 409 scenario as a side-effect of the concurrent-add fix. |
| 2026-06-22 | **Activator principle.** The person who creates a space carries NO ongoing authority over the group — only the structural responsibility of being the un-removable anchor. Equal authority within the group is foundational to "Our*" products; the moment one person retains ongoing control it becomes a different product. |
| 2026-06-22 | **Attribution vs. ownership rule (Harbour-wide).** Attribution is free and universal (who created an item/category/household, shown plainly). | 2026-06-22 | **Membership model = text-group model.** ANY member can add anyone and remove ANY non-creator (incl. self = leave). Creator cannot be removed by anyone and cannot leave — must delete the household to exit. Overrides the earlier "remove is owner-only" brief. |
| 2026-06-22 | **Keep `role = 'owner'` in the schema (do NOT rename to `creator`).** The seat is expected to accrue real responsibility later; `owner` is the honest name for "anchor that runs flat today, may carry weight tomorrow." Stage 3: guarded QA↔Claude Code autonomous fix loop — agents edit app code but NOT the tests; each fix a reviewable commit; bounded retries then escalate. Stage 4: handoff/Scribe records pipeline provenance. Principle: automate deterministic toil first, judgment last; keep a human at the prod (irreversible) boundary. |

| 2026-07-09 | **False-removal-banner P0 root cause = deliberate-loss / watchdog-poll race, not a transient-empty read.** The 30s `checkPresence` poll and the deliberate delete/leave handlers both react to one membership loss; the poll fires the loud notice during the reconciliation gap. Instrumentation (a temporary DIAG capture) disproved the transient-empty theory and the staple-churn correlation — churn only mattered because long sessions cross poll boundaries. |
| 2026-07-09 | **Guard deliberate-loss at the handler layer, BEFORE the destructive RPC — not inside the resolver.** v1 set `resolvingRef` on resolver entry, but the RPC runs first (~2s on 3G), leaving an unguarded window a poll can land in. v2 raises `deliberateLossRef` at the start of the handler and clears in `finally`, spanning RPC + resolution. Mechanism B (self-clearing ref) at timing A (before the action). The durable rule: a background watchdog must be guarded across the *whole* deliberate action, from before its first async boundary. |

Server idempotency (fuzzy `(created_by, name)`-within-a-window guard) stays a WATCH item, promoted only if a non-human `created_at` signature appears (retry hook defeating the button). The idle timer means the link is normally ready by Send-tap time; the tap path guarantees a generate if the user beats the timer. `prepareInvite` is idempotent (guards on url/in-flight) so timer + tap can't double-write. |

The full invite + link ride in `text` across every target, so nothing is lost. A `mailto:` fallback (guaranteed subject) is a future "we heard you" beat only if email becomes a primary invite channel — not pre-launch work. |
| 2026-07-10 | **Browse un-added rows show "Add", not a zero-quantity stepper.** Invariant: on-list ⇔ qty > 0 ⇔ stepper; not-on-list ⇔ qty 0 ⇔ "Add". Zero is never rendered — kills the ambiguous zero-state and quiets most rows to one word. Honors the boundary `updateQty` already enforces (`Math.max(0, qty)`, `qty <= 0` = removal). |
| 2026-07-10 | **Stepper and Add share one ghost-pill treatment** (transparent-on-cream, clay-lo `#C9A97A` outline, no white fill, no shadow). The "pill sticks out" problem was really visual-weight mismatch: bright white stepper vs. transparent Add. Matching them makes the two states read as one control family. |

| 2026-07-10 | **Spec lifecycle: authored → `active/` → `built/` (on ship) → `retired/` (if superseded).** Built specs are archived as forensic record, not deleted — the truth table / rejected alternatives / "why" are the best record when a shipped feature later breaks. Retired ≠ built: retired = never-shipped-or-dead-diagnostic; built = live in product. Kept separate so `built/` is a trustworthy map of shipped behavior. *(Velayo OS convention — reorg DESIGNED, not yet executed; see NEXT.)* |

(`.app` remains the parked future canonical-front-door plan, not live prod — see the `Jun 29` `.app`-parked decision.) |
| 2026-07-10 | **At declutter merge: RETIRE `SPEC_filter_show_hide.md`** (superseded by the declutter cycle) — do not leave it in `active/` once the reorg runs. |

Design analogue of "truth is a query against the correct environment, never the doc." |

Verified: an incoming check/uncheck re-derives the hide filter off live checked-state without resetting phase. Generalizes to any future per-device view control over shared data. |

Persisting flat would silently strip grouping until the user noticed. Removed `op_showCategories`; Shop's view mode is now derived from cycle phase. |

One goal per session; bug-fix (build) and nav/affordances (design) pull in opposite directions, so they're sequenced, not merged. |

Two-part fix to ratify in a design session: (a) visible affordance for critical actions, not only a gesture; (b) one reusable milestone-keyed coachmark primitive (not per-feature one-offs). |

Sacandaga framing = **Slice B (middle)**: cottage, flag, water line. Must include a **framing/reposition step at import** — vertical photos need a user-chosen horizontal slice; the app can't guess lake-vs-faces. |

## DONE — Shipped & Live

### Solo-start + earned-Our wordmark SHIPPED TO PROD — one release, migration first ✓ *(2026-08-30; `5304a4e`; closes the P0 promotion row and the P1 wordmark build)*

The 2026-08-24 Prem/Cyrus incident is now designed away **in production**. Both halves of the solo narrative shipped together, which is what makes it read as one idea: the header says "Provisions" while the welcome sheet asks what to call your first place.

**Executed in the required order, and the order was the point:**

1. **`042` applied to the PROD database first.** The client sends `p_ref_code` whenever a `?ref=` code is present, so a client-first deploy would have returned **PGRST202** on every referral arrival. Verified after the fact by direct query against prod (`parpauldmbetptkmdwbd`): `bootstrap_new_user(p_email, p_invite_code, p_full_name, p_ref_code)`, `discard_unclaimed_household(uuid)`, `mint_referral_code()`, and both `users.referral_code` / `users.referred_by` present.
2. **`dev→main`**, both branches now at `5304a4e`, origin in sync.
3. **Live-verified on `ourprovisions.velayo.ai`** with a real two-account invite/join — from the app, not the SQL editor. The header updated **live and in place on an already-open tab, with no refresh**, which is the earned-Our mechanic doing exactly what it was specified to do.

**Earned-Our wordmark** (`5304a4e`) — `hasEarnedOur = isSignedIn && householdMembers.length > 1` is the single source of truth. Behaviour before label falls out of the data rather than needing a guard: `householdMembers` starts `[]` and fills per active household, so "Provisions" is the default while loading, when solo, and when signed out. The splash→header travel/clone hand-off is **deleted** — `headerTitleRef`, `.op-handoff`, `@keyframes opHandoff`, `--op-dx/dy`, and the `opacity: showSplash ? 0` gate — confirmed zero references by grep, and absent from the prod bundle. The splash wordmark still reads the full "OurProvisions": it is a brand surface, and the app's name is not conditional.

**One deliberate deviation, recorded rather than silently made.** The spec said the transition is "an opacity fade (no text swap → no layout shift)". Read literally — a phantom zero-opacity "Our" still occupying its width — the *solo* header would sit permanently off-centre in a `text-align: center` band, a visible defect in the exact case the fix exists to serve. It transitions `max-width` alongside `opacity` instead: no text swap, no reflow of "Provisions", correctly centred at both ends. Intent over literal wording; see DECISIONS.

**This closes the five-week loop.** §8 of `SPEC_splash_vessel_identity_v2.md` standardised the header to always-"Our" so the hand-off would look seamless. That spec was retired 2026-07-25 and **the header change it mandated was never reverted with it** — which is why a correct solo signup read as "a stranger is in my household" a month later. A retired spec's *behaviour* does not retire itself.

### Solo-start experience — naming sheet, invite recovery, two share verbs, referral carry ✓ *(2026-08-26; **DEV ONLY — not on prod**; `dcae2e0`→`36595c6`; spec: `docs/specs/built/SPEC_solo_start_experience.md`)*

The 2026-08-24 Prem/Cyrus incident is designed away, and the design was verified rather than assumed: **all 10 of the spec's verification items passed on dev**, each confirmed by screenshot and/or DB query, and **the originating incident was re-run as an unscripted regression check** — a named place, no silent `"My Household"` confusion.

- **Migration `042`** (`dcae2e0`) — `users.referral_code`/`referred_by`, `bootstrap_new_user` gains `p_ref_code` and returns `created_household`, plus the `discard_unclaimed_household` D11 guard. Triple-verified on dev: signature, a full source read of **all three return branches**, and both columns present.
- **The welcome sheet fires on a conjunction, not a flag** — `just_signed_up` ∧ no `?invite=` ∧ the `"My Household"` sentinel. Verification item 10 (an existing user signing *in*, including via `?ref=`) never renders it.
- **D11 confirmed BIDIRECTIONALLY, which is the half that is easy to skip.** An empty sentinel place is soft-deleted on code-join (4a); a place holding **one real item survives the identical join** (4b). Both tested with separate accounts. A guard only proven in the deleting direction is not proven.
- **Item 9 confirmed a branching referral tree**, not merely a chain: dan→test303→{test304, test306}.

**Independently re-verified 2026-08-30 — items 4, 6 and 7, from a second machine that could not see this record.** A stale `origin/dev` led the 2026-08-29 catch-up to believe the items were unrun, so the three DB-settleable ones were run again against `supabase-dev` (project `zxwtxjjmssykhqrghouf`, `system_identifier` `7642734024280108049`, proved distinct from prod’s `7606130613603586966` before anything was read). **All three agree with the 2026-08-26 result:**

- **Item 4** — `test305`’s sentinel place was soft-deleted **163 ms** after the code-join (07:00:58.307 → 07:00:58.470, membership cascaded); `test309`’s place, holding one item added **48 s before** its join, survived with `deleted_at` NULL. The control’s ordering was checked in the data, not assumed.
- **Item 6** — zero invite rows by the ref-share sender, zero in either ref-arrival window, and zero ever for any household owned by the four ref-arrived users; `handleAppShare` (`App.js:2116-2138`) issues no Supabase call at all.
- **Item 7** — four `referred_by` rows resolving dan→test300, dan→test303, test303→{test304, test306}; each referee is `owner` of their own same-instant household and a member of nothing else. `users_referral_code_key` UNIQUE index confirmed; the self-referral predicate returns 0 against each user’s own code.

### Create / edit meal — PLAN is user-populatable ✓ *(2026-08-20; **DEV ONLY — not on prod**; `0fd9c39`→`ab4546a`; spec: `docs/specs/built/SPEC_create_meal_ui.md`)*

**The demo plan's named failure mode is closed: a meal can now be created from inside the app.** One parameterized `MealSheet` serves both create and edit — built as one component from the start rather than retrofitted, which is the decision that made edit nearly free. `updateMeal` was added to `useProvisions.js`, mirroring `createMeal`'s plain-write shape (update the row, blanket soft-delete + reinsert ingredients). **No migration, no schema change.**

**Verified end to end on the dev preview under real Clerk auth against real data** — create including an ingredient invented through the no-results panel, edit, edit-doesn't-touch-already-listed-items, and `+ New category`.

**Meal rows now swipe to reveal Edit**, matching catalog rows, with "Add" staying on the row face as the primary one-tap action. That required generalizing `SwipeToRemove` into an N-action wrapper first.

> **⚠️ Four rounds of post-verification polish, and the pattern in them is worth keeping.** Each round traced to a real cause rather than being tuned by eye: a `+ New category` tile shipped as a **dead control** (passed a no-op stub); row and stepper styling where **the mockup had invented a visual language beside Browse's shipped one**; and finally the stepper's missing `.qty-controls` wrapper — **found by DOM diff only after two attempts reasoning from source landed close but wrong.** The lesson recorded: when a component reproduces an existing one, compare the rendered DOM, not the source.
>
> **⚠️ Two swipe regressions, both introduced by this work, both diagnosed to root cause.** (1) `SWIPE_THRESHOLD` was a flat 60px tuned when panels were always 240px; at 80px it became a **75% dead zone**, so short drags snapped the row back open — it was never failing to register the gesture, it was **actively re-opening**. Fixed by scaling the threshold to the panel, scoped to the edit branch so the destructive swipe-to-remove path kept its 60px. (2) A **stale-closure read** — gesture logic read `offsetX` state while `move` events are continuous-priority in React 18, so a discrete `mouseup` could run before the last move committed, and `handleStart` then seeded the next gesture from the bad value, compounding until it wedged. Now mirrored in a ref.

Deliberate: a one-file folder standing next to a peer document still at root would have created **two conventions for the same kind of doc** — the outcome the decision existed to avoid. `SPEC_docs_reorg.md`'s 2026-07-11 line *"stays at docs/ root for now (evidence, not a spec)"* is now superseded; **left unedited on purpose** — it is a built spec and an accurate record of what was decided then.

### Cycle integrity `038` + the meals migrations `025`/`026` — **LIVE ON PROD** ✓ *(2026-08-18; `038` dev 2026-08-17, prod 2026-08-18; spec: `docs/specs/built/SPEC_cycle_integrity_038.md`)*

**Applied as one prod batch, step-by-step, with independent read-only verification between every step** — the repair one-off, the index bare, the enforcement fixture, `025`, `026`. **No step was accepted on "no error";** each was confirmed by a separate query run from outside the SQL editor.

**`uq_open_cycle_per_household`** is live on both environments — more than one open cycle per household is now **structurally impossible**, not merely unobserved. Proven behaviourally rather than by catalogue inspection: the fixture **attempts the violation** (rejected), then closes the first cycle and opens another (**accepted**) to prove the predicate is partial and close-then-open still works.

**Prod data repaired:** `Our calendar`'s two open cycles merged keeping the 14-item survivor (now 16), the emptied cycle **closed rather than deleted**, and the 2 stranded `Lake house` rows repointed into that household's open cycle. All four share one `updated_at`; `rolled_from_item_id` left NULL so no roll-forward provenance was invented.

**The `025`/`026` prod gate — open since 2026-07-28 — is closed.** Meals is live infrastructure on prod: three tables, RLS with **11** policies (all `{authenticated}`, zero `anon`), `add_meal_to_list` with `anon` EXECUTE revoked, plus `026`'s resurrect trigger and the server-side cycle resolution in `insert_list_item` (replaced at exactly one overload — no `bootstrap_new_user`-style signature collision). This also removes the hazard of nine meals commits on `main` running against a prod DB with no `meals` tables.

> **(1) The 2026-07-31 "residue" premise was wrong.** That census found 2 stranded rows; at point-of-build prod held **18 across 4 live households**, closes spanning 2026-06-07 → **2026-08-08**. Most predate 07-31 — the census under-counted. **(2) `close_cycle`'s "Bug A — no evidence" deferral is reversed:** 16 of the 18 are exactly that close-side shape, confirmed in the live function body, still firing. Those rows were **deliberately left in place as evidence** and the fix is its own NEXT item. **(3) `insert_list_item` is `anon`-executable with no membership guard** — found while reviewing `026`, pre-existing, now its own NEXT item.
>
> **✅ §F5 CLOSED — by database-side substitute, and the distinction matters.** The spec's *"open the app as a real user"* check **could not run as written: neither operator is a member of `Our calendar`** — it belongs to Christopher and Heddi. Substituted a `list_items` → `catalog_items` join over the survivor cycle: **16 live rows, no duplicates, sane quantities and status, and the 2 repointed rows carrying the repair's `2026-08-18 03:43:42` stamp while the other 14 retained their original `2026-07-16`/`07-24` stamps.** That is direct proof the repair moved exactly the intended rows and nothing else. `get_list_items_for_household` independently returns 16. ⚠️ **It proves the DATA, not the rendered UI** — a substitute, not an equivalent. **First-party confirmation is still owed**, and Christopher and Heddi are conveniently the same two people whose cutover sign-in is already outstanding, so one action closes both gates.


### Authorization Parts 5, 3 and 2 — **THE ENTIRE RLS/RPC SERIES IS CLOSED** ✓ *(2026-08-17; **LIVE ON DEV + PROD**, every part verified from outside the SQL editor)*

**Parts 0, 1, 2, 3, 4a, 4b and 5 of `docs/specs/built/SPEC_rls_and_rpc_authorization.md` are all shipped.** Tonight closed the last three.

| Part | Migration | What it closed |
|---|---|---|
| **5** | `034_pin_search_path_secdef.sql` | Pinned `search_path = public, extensions` on the five remaining SECURITY DEFINER functions. `proconfig is null` → **0 in both environments**. Behaviour-neutral, checked before authoring: none of the five bodies references `auth.*`, `storage.*`, `extensions.*` or earthdistance. |
| **3** | `035_drop_household_members_insert.sql` | Dropped `household_members_insert` (`WITH CHECK (true)` — any authenticated caller could self-insert into any household at `role: 'owner'`). `household_members` is now **SELECT-only** at the client layer; membership is RPC-owned. |
| **2** | `036_bootstrap_new_user_derive_clerk_id.sql` + `037_drop_bootstrap_new_user_4arg.sql` | `bootstrap_new_user` no longer accepts `p_clerk_id`; identity derives from `auth.jwt()->>'sub'`. Closed the anon-key path that could overwrite any user's email/name and, with any valid invite, join a household **as them**. |

**Client change:** `useProvisions.js:312-317` drops `p_clerk_id` from the RPC call (`1ebe263`, `dev` → `main` fast-forward). Only source change in the whole 17-commit merge range; the rest is docs and inert migration files.

**Verification that mattered, and why:**
- **Part 3's safety was verified, not assumed** — a SECURITY DEFINER function bypasses RLS only if its owner is exempt, which needs table ownership **and** `relforcerowsecurity = false`. Both confirmed. **Had FORCE RLS been set, `035` would have locked every user out of joining a household.**
- **Part 2 ran as a zero-downtime split** (`036` → client deploy → `037`) because dropping a signature breaks every in-flight caller, and bootstrap runs on **every sign-in**, not just signups.
- **Three cold sign-ups on dev with genuinely fresh identities:** plain (clean household, correct `owner`), valid-invite (joined as second member), and **expired-invite falling through to a fresh household with a graceful toast** — the flagged regression risk, since raising instead of falling through rolls back the user upsert and leaves a new user unable to sign up at all.
- **Closure proved from outside the database.** The obvious check — anon `POST {"p_email":…}` → `42501` — passes **identically before and after** and is worthless alone. The evidence was the forged shape `{"p_clerk_id":"000…0", …}` returning `PGRST202`, plus a **control call** firing the identical forged shape at `create_household`, which reached the function body and raised `P0001` — proving the block is signature-specific, not a broken RPC.
- **Prod advisor:** `anon_security_definer_function_executable` **25 → 21**, `function_search_path_mutable` **5 → 0**, cross-checked against `pg_proc` (`anon_secdef 21 / auth_secdef 23 / unpinned 0`) rather than eye-counting lint entries.

⚠️ **Opened by this work, tracked separately:** `create_household` carries the identical Part 2 defect (NEXT), and 21 `anon`-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions remain (LATER). Neither is in this spec's scope.

### Authorization Part 1 — `join_household` validates the invite server-side ✓ *(2026-07-31; **DEV ONLY — not on prod**)*

| Feature | Date | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **`030_join_household_invite_validation.sql`** | Jul 31, 2026 (**dev only**) | `join_household(uuid)` **dropped** for `join_household(p_invite_code text) returns json {household_id, household_name, revived}`; `anon`/`PUBLIC` EXECUTE revoked; `invites_select` re-scoped `qual = true` → `is_member_of(household_id)`; `anon` table grants revoked. All one transaction (`db5ec66`). **The exploit needed no out-of-band code:** `invites_select` returned every invite row in the system, and `join_household` took the bare `household_id` those rows handed over — **9 of 23 live prod households (39%)** reachable by anyone who could sign up. `live_redeemable = 0` was not protection, because the RPC never consulted invite state. **Both halves shipped together** — either alone leaves a working attack. Virginia · 5 min · assert 200 · uptime detector <90%, 2 consecutive violations, Critical. Exercises **PostgREST**, not the Vercel HTML shell (which stayed green through the outage). | **F1b — hide→re-add no longer stomps shared quantity** | Jul 15, 2026 | Hide is a lens, not an edit. `searchResults` excludes hidden items, so a hidden-but-live item showed "No results" and the "Add" verb routed through `updateQty(name,1)` — stomping the shared qty 10→1 across the person boundary. Fix (client-only, `85f4a69`): new `unhideItem` un-hide-only primitive (never touches `list_items`); `updateQty` create branch resolves a hidden exact-normalized name to the existing catalog row (hidden ≠ new); search surfaces a hidden-but-live exact match as a **reveal card** (un-hide only, no qty write) instead of a false "add as new." | **Contributor-attribution display fix (A-fix) — VERIFIED + MERGED ✓** | Jul 16, 2026 | DH saw "Dan Test User" on items whose only contributor was Dan Holmes: `list_items.added_by` is immutable (INSERT-only) and drifts from `list_item_contributors`; the `≤1 contributor` UI branch fell back to it. Shop name-line now derives from the ledger — `contributors[0].fullName` when exactly one contributor, nothing when zero; `isOwnItem` keys off the sole contributor's `clerkId` (`3182afc`, App.js). **Dev-verified two-account against all three assertions, merged dev→main (`d972c88` → `abc5f15`), prod-verified 2026-07-16.** Test-script correction: there is no "remove" button — the remove path fires from `updateQty` at qty 0, and only the *other* user's window can read the result (`isOwnItem` suppresses your own name line). Remove-confirm dialog still reads `added_by` (repoint later, with the build-gated ledger work). |

|---|---|---|
| **Bug 1 — duplicate catalog item on re-add (catalog fork)** | Jul 13, 2026 | Prod query overturned the list-layer hypothesis: 3 list rows pointed at 3 distinct `catalog_item_id`s → the **catalog** was forking. Fixed at write-time by migration **018** (`insert_custom_catalog_item` rewritten sql→plpgsql, idempotent: reuse a live same-name row matched on normalized name — lower+trim+collapse-ws — prefer global then oldest custom, store original casing) + DB backstop migration **019** (partial unique indexes `uq_global_catalog_norm` + `uq_custom_catalog_norm`). Census found + cleaned **12 fork sets** on prod (10 global double-seed dups Mar-20/Mar-30 prod-only, 2 custom) via reversible soft-delete/merge; 0 remaining. Both migrations applied dev+prod, `.sql` records committed (`dcdf2bc`, `7173f6a`). |
| **Bug 2 — check one, both check** | Jul 13, 2026 | `toggleChecked` wrote `.eq("catalog_item_id", …)`, flipping every live row sharing that catalog id. Now writes `.eq("id", listItemId)` (unique row); `listItemId` threaded `newListRows[].id` → `shoppingList` item → all four `toggleChecked` callsites. Commit `b85dcbf`. |
| **Bug 3 — toggle bounce / ~3 taps to stick** | Jul 13, 2026 | The 2s poll called `setChecked(newChecked)` unconditionally, clobbering the optimistic value before the write committed. Added `pendingCheckRef` (mirror of `pendingQtyRef`): `toggleChecked` marks the item in-flight before the optimistic set and clears on settle; `loadListItems` preserves `prev[name]` for in-flight names. Commit `b85dcbf`; prod-verified incl. Slow-3G stress. |
